<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?><rss version="2.0" xmlns:content="http://purl.org/rss/1.0/modules/content/" xmlns:atom="http://www.w3.org/2005/Atom" xmlns:fh="http://purl.org/syndication/history/1.0"><channel><title>Tauri | Blog</title><description>The cross-platform app building toolkit</description><link>https://v2.tauri.app/</link><language>en</language><atom:link rel="self" href="https://v2.tauri.app/blog/rss.xml"/><atom:link rel="prev-archive" href="https://v2.tauri.app/blog/rss/2026-06.xml"/><item><title>Tauri Board Elections 2026</title><link>https://v2.tauri.app/blog/tauri-board-elections-2026/</link><guid isPermaLink="true">https://v2.tauri.app/blog/tauri-board-elections-2026/</guid><description>Time flies and it is time for a new round of elections for the next term.</description><pubDate>Tue, 30 Jun 2026 00:00:00 GMT</pubDate><content:encoded>&lt;p&gt;As we continue to be amazed by all the applications you’ve created with Tauri v2, it’s been 5 years since Tauri has become a Programme within &lt;a href=&quot;https://commonsconservancy.org/&quot;&gt;The Commons Conservancy&lt;/a&gt;! This means we’re preparing for a new round of Tauri Board Director elections for the next term.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h2 id=&quot;board-elections&quot;&gt;Board Elections&lt;/h2&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The Tauri Board of Directors is the central decision-making body for the Tauri Programme and is responsible for its overall health and stability. Additional details can be found at the &lt;a href=&quot;https://v2.tauri.app/about/governance/&quot;&gt;Tauri Governance page&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;In order to provide continuity from one year to another, the elections of seats are staggered over the course of 2 years. This means that one year a portion of the seats are elected, and the following year the remaining seats are elected. A Tauri Director seat is an elected position with a term of 2 years. The Board has a minimum of 3 Directors and a maximum of 7.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;This year there are 3 seats open for election.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h3 id=&quot;applying-for-candidacy&quot;&gt;Applying for Candidacy&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;There are 3 steps to express interest and apply for candidacy:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ol&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Learn about the role of a Tauri Board Director on the &lt;a href=&quot;https://v2.tauri.app/about/governance/#tauri-board--board-directors&quot;&gt;Governance page&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Prepare a written introduction about yourself that covers who you are, your history and relevance to Tauri, and what you would like to bring to the Board. (Examples from previous years can be found at &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/governance-and-guidance&quot;&gt;https://github.com/tauri-apps/governance-and-guidance&lt;/a&gt;).&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Apply prior to July 7th, 2026&lt;/strong&gt; by emailing &lt;a href=&quot;mailto:board@tauri.app&quot;&gt;board@tauri.app&lt;/a&gt; or messaging the &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;@board&lt;/code&gt; role on the &lt;a href=&quot;https://discord.com/invite/tauri&quot;&gt;Tauri Discord&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ol&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Voting will take place starting on July 7th through July 14th, 2026 where Tauri Working Group Members will cast their votes. We’re expecting to announce the results on or before July 19, 2026. If elected, we’ll ask you to sign &lt;a href=&quot;https://dracc.commonsconservancy.org/0016/&quot;&gt;this pledge&lt;/a&gt; if you haven’t done so previously.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;If you have any questions or would like more information please reach out by emailing &lt;a href=&quot;mailto:board@tauri.app&quot;&gt;board@tauri.app&lt;/a&gt;, messaging the &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;@board&lt;/code&gt; role on the &lt;a href=&quot;https://discord.com/invite/tauri&quot;&gt;Tauri Discord&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h2 id=&quot;interested-in-getting-involved&quot;&gt;Interested In Getting Involved?&lt;/h2&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;If joining the Board isn’t for you but still want to help, here are some ways to do so! (Thank you ❤️)&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Reach out on our &lt;a href=&quot;https://discord.com/invite/tauri&quot;&gt;Discord&lt;/a&gt; by enabling the roles you’re interested in like “Contribute to Tauri Code” or “Contribute to Documentation”. This reveals the Contributors channels where you can chat with other contributors. While you’re there, consider enabling Discord notification roles to stay apprised of news and questions from the team.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Or on GitHub maybe you can help with these &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/search?q=user%3Atauri-apps+label%3A%22good+first+issue%22+state%3Aopen&amp;#x26;type=Issues&amp;#x26;s=created&amp;#x26;o=desc&quot;&gt;good first issues&lt;/a&gt;?&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Additionally we’re very grateful to all the teams and individuals who &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/sponsors/tauri-apps&quot;&gt;sponsor us on GitHub&lt;/a&gt; or &lt;a href=&quot;https://opencollective.com/tauri&quot;&gt;open collective&lt;/a&gt;!&lt;/p&gt;
</content:encoded></item><item><title>Tauri Board Elections 2025</title><link>https://v2.tauri.app/blog/tauri-board-elections-2025/</link><guid isPermaLink="true">https://v2.tauri.app/blog/tauri-board-elections-2025/</guid><description>As we continue to be amazed with all the applications you&apos;ve created with Tauri v2, it&apos;s been 4 years that Tauri has become a Programme within The Commons Conservancy! This means we&apos;re preparing for a new round of Tauri Board Director elections for the next term.</description><pubDate>Mon, 30 Jun 2025 00:00:00 GMT</pubDate><content:encoded>&lt;p&gt;As we continue to be amazed by all the applications you’ve created with Tauri v2, it’s been 4 years since Tauri has become a Programme within &lt;a href=&quot;https://commonsconservancy.org/&quot;&gt;The Commons Conservancy&lt;/a&gt;! This means we’re preparing for a new round of Tauri Board Director elections for the next term.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h2 id=&quot;board-elections&quot;&gt;Board Elections&lt;/h2&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The Tauri Board of Directors is the central decision-making body for the Tauri Programme and is responsible for its overall health and stability. Additional details can be found at the &lt;a href=&quot;https://v2.tauri.app/about/governance/&quot;&gt;Tauri Governance page&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;In order to provide continuity from one year to another, the elections of seats are staggered over the course of 2 years. This means that one year a portion of the seats are elected, and the following year the remaining seats are elected. A Tauri Director seat is an elected position with a term of 2 years. The Board has a minimum of 3 Directors and a maximum of 7.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;This year there are 5 seats open for election.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h3 id=&quot;applying-for-candidacy&quot;&gt;Applying for Candidacy&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;There are 3 steps to express interest and apply for candidacy:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ol&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Learn about the role of a Tauri Board Director on the &lt;a href=&quot;https://v2.tauri.app/about/governance/#tauri-board--board-directors&quot;&gt;Governance page&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Prepare a written introduction about yourself that covers who you are, your history and relevance to Tauri, and what you would like to bring to the Board. (Examples from previous years can be found at &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/governance-and-guidance&quot;&gt;https://github.com/tauri-apps/governance-and-guidance&lt;/a&gt;).&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Apply prior to July 7th, 2025&lt;/strong&gt; by emailing &lt;a href=&quot;mailto:board@tauri.app&quot;&gt;board@tauri.app&lt;/a&gt; or messaging the &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;@board&lt;/code&gt; role on the &lt;a href=&quot;https://discord.com/invite/tauri&quot;&gt;Tauri Discord&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ol&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Voting will take place starting on July 7th through July 14th, 2025 where Tauri Working Group Members will cast their votes. We’re expecting to announce the results on or before July 19, 2025. If elected, we’ll ask you to sign &lt;a href=&quot;https://dracc.commonsconservancy.org/0016/&quot;&gt;this pledge&lt;/a&gt; if you haven’t done so previously.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;If you have any questions or would like more information please reach out by emailing &lt;a href=&quot;mailto:board@tauri.app&quot;&gt;board@tauri.app&lt;/a&gt;, messaging the &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;@board&lt;/code&gt; role on the &lt;a href=&quot;https://discord.com/invite/tauri&quot;&gt;Tauri Discord&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h2 id=&quot;interested-in-getting-involved&quot;&gt;Interested In Getting Involved?&lt;/h2&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;If joining the Board isn’t for you but still want to help, here are some ways to do so! (Thank you ❤️)&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Reach out on our &lt;a href=&quot;https://discord.com/invite/tauri&quot;&gt;Discord&lt;/a&gt; by enabling the roles you’re interested in like “Contribute to Tauri Code” or “Contribute to Documentation”. This reveals the Contributors channels where you can chat with other contributors. While you’re there, consider enabling Discord notification roles to stay apprised of news and questions from the team.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Or on GitHub maybe you can help with these &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/search?q=user%3Atauri-apps+label%3A%22good+first+issue%22+state%3Aopen&amp;#x26;type=Issues&amp;#x26;s=created&amp;#x26;o=desc&quot;&gt;good first issues&lt;/a&gt;?&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Additionally we’re very grateful to all the teams and individuals who &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/sponsors/tauri-apps&quot;&gt;sponsor us on GitHub&lt;/a&gt; or &lt;a href=&quot;https://opencollective.com/tauri&quot;&gt;open collective&lt;/a&gt;!&lt;/p&gt;
</content:encoded></item><item><title>Experimental Tauri Verso Integration</title><link>https://v2.tauri.app/blog/tauri-verso-integration/</link><guid isPermaLink="true">https://v2.tauri.app/blog/tauri-verso-integration/</guid><description>The first look of the integration with Verso, a browser based on Servo</description><pubDate>Mon, 17 Mar 2025 00:00:00 GMT</pubDate><content:encoded>&lt;div&gt;&lt;h2 id=&quot;what-is-verso&quot;&gt;What is Verso?&lt;/h2&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;So first off, what is &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;Verso&lt;/code&gt;? &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/versotile-org/verso&quot;&gt;&lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;Verso&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/a&gt; is a browser based on &lt;a href=&quot;https://servo.org/&quot;&gt;&lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;Servo&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, a web browser rendering engine written in Rust&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h2 id=&quot;why-using-verso-instead-of-servo-directly&quot;&gt;Why using Verso instead of Servo directly?&lt;/h2&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;I believe there’re quite a lot of people having thought about using Servo but got intimidated by the complex APIs and just gave up, which frankly I was one of them, so the goal of building the Verso webview is to make it easy enough to understand and use so that people will actually start to experiment and use it&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Servo itself is made to be relatively easy to embed compared to other browsers, but the APIs are still way too low level and it’s quite daunting to use, you can take a look at the minimal example for running Servo with Winit at (note this is not even a fully functional example): &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/servo/servo/blob/8d39d7706aee50971e848a5e31fc6bfd7ef552c1/components/servo/examples/winit_minimal.rs&quot;&gt;https://github.com/servo/servo/blob/8d39d7706aee50971e848a5e31fc6bfd7ef552c1/components/servo/examples/winit_minimal.rs&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;And compared to that, Verso’s API looks like this, which is much easier and ergonomic to use&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;figure&gt;&lt;figcaption&gt;&lt;/figcaption&gt;&lt;pre&gt;&lt;code&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;use&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; std&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;::&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;env&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;::&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;current_exe;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;use&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; std&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;::&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;thread&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;::&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;sleep;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;use&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; std&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;::&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;time&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;::&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;Duration;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;use&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; url&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;::&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;Url;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;use&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; verso&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;::&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;VersoBuilder;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;
&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;fn&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;main&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;() {&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;    &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;let&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;versoview_path&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;=&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;current_exe&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;()&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;.&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;unwrap&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;()&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;.&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;parent&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;()&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;.&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;unwrap&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;()&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;.&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;join&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;(&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;&quot;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;versoview&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;&quot;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;);&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;    &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;let&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;controller&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;=&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; VersoBuilder&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;::&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;new&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;()&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;        &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;.&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;with_panel&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;(&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;true&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;)&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;        &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;.&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;maximized&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;(&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;true&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;)&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;        &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;.&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;build&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;(&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;versoview_path&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;, Url&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;::&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;parse&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;(&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;&quot;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;https://example.com&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;&quot;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;)&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;.&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;unwrap&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;());&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;    &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;loop&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; {&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;        &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;sleep&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;(Duration&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;::&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;MAX&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;);&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;&lt;span&gt;    &lt;/span&gt;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;}&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;}&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/figure&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;blockquote&gt;
&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/versotile-org/verso/blob/2e853d4f3f4cb88274daa211b7a2eb3bd1517115/verso/src/main.rs&quot;&gt;https://github.com/versotile-org/verso/blob/2e853d4f3f4cb88274daa211b7a2eb3bd1517115/verso/src/main.rs&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/blockquote&gt;
&lt;p&gt;It’s not to say Servo’s API is bad though, as they need to support a lot more use cases while we just need it for building applications with Tauri&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h2 id=&quot;tauri-runtime-verso&quot;&gt;&lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri-runtime-verso&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/h2&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;So let’s talk about the integration with Tauri!&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;We choose to integrate Verso and Tauri through a new custom runtime &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/versotile-org/tauri-runtime-verso&quot;&gt;&lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri-runtime-verso&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/a&gt; for this time, this is similar to our default runtime &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri-runtime-wry&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;With this approach, you can easily swap out the runtime and use Tauri like what you’ll normally do:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;figure&gt;&lt;figcaption&gt;&lt;/figcaption&gt;&lt;pre&gt;&lt;code&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;use&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; tauri_runtime_verso&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;::&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;{&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;&lt;span&gt;    &lt;/span&gt;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;INVOKE_SYSTEM_SCRIPTS, VersoRuntime, set_verso_path, set_verso_resource_directory,&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;};&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;
&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;fn&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;main&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;() {&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;    &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;// You need to set this to the path of the versoview executable&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;    &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;// before creating any of the webview windows&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;    &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;set_verso_path&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;(&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;&quot;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;../verso/target/debug/versoview&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;&quot;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;);&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;    &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;// Set this to verso/servo&apos;s resources directory before creating any of the webview windows&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;    &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;// this is optional but recommended, this directory will include very important things&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;    &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;// like user agent stylesheet&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;    &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;set_verso_resource_directory&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;(&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;&quot;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;../verso/resources&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;&quot;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;);&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;&lt;span&gt;    &lt;/span&gt;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;tauri&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;::&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;Builder&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;::&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;&amp;#x3C;VersoRuntime&gt;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;::&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;new&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;()&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;        &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;// Make sure to do this or some of the commands will not work&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;        &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;.&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;invoke_system&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;(&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;INVOKE_SYSTEM_SCRIPTS&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;.&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;to_owned&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;())&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;        &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;.&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;run&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;(tauri&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;::&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;generate_context!&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;())&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;        &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;.&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;unwrap&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;();&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;}&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/figure&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Just note that it’s not as feature rich and powerful as the current backends used by Tauri in production yet, but it still has a lot to it, and we have built an example show casing it at &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/versotile-org/tauri-runtime-verso/tree/main/examples/api&quot;&gt;https://github.com/versotile-org/tauri-runtime-verso/tree/main/examples/api&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;&lt;video controls src=&quot;/assets/blog/verso-integration/dev-show-case.mp4&quot; title=&quot;Verso integration show case&quot;&gt;&lt;/video&gt;&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h4 id=&quot;features-you-can-see-from-the-video&quot;&gt;Features you can see from the video:&lt;/h4&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
&lt;li&gt;We have all the functions the &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri-cli&lt;/code&gt; provides&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;We’re using a modern framework, in this case &lt;a href=&quot;https://react.dev/&quot;&gt;&lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;React&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;We have our official log and opener plugins, they work exactly the same as if you’re using Tauri with the other backends&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Windowing functions work, including size, position, maximize, minimize, close, …&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;&lt;a href=&quot;https://vitejs.dev/&quot;&gt;&lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;Vite&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/a&gt;’s css hot reload works as well&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;The &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;data-tauri-drag-region&lt;/code&gt; attribute works&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h2 id=&quot;future-works&quot;&gt;Future works&lt;/h2&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Right now, Verso and &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri-runtime-verso&lt;/code&gt; are still in active development so we’ll need to see as we go, but we do have something planned to do next&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h3 id=&quot;pre-built-verso-executable&quot;&gt;Pre-built Verso executable&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Releasing an easy to use pre-built Verso executable to help people get started with it quicker and easier, as currently you need to compile Verso yourself to get started&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Also if possible, as a long term goal, we would like an evergreen shared Verso, similar to WebView2 on Windows which you would place it on the system and it would update itself automatically, and shared between multiple apps so you don’t have to ship the browser inside your app to reduce the bundle size significantly&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h3 id=&quot;more-windowing-and-webview-features-support&quot;&gt;More windowing and webview features support&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;We currently only support a small subset of features in Tauri, and we would like to expand this to include more things, and we have currently planned to support window decorations, window titles and transparency&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h3 id=&quot;initialization-script-without-temporary-files&quot;&gt;Initialization script without temporary files&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Currently Servo can only take an userscript directory to run on document start which is ok but for the Tauri’s use case, we would like to do this programmatically without the help of files, as that could result in left over temporary files that we never clean up&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;We have a &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/35388&quot;&gt;PR&lt;/a&gt; merged in Servo just a few days ago and we should just need to use in Verso and then the &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tarui-runtime-verso&lt;/code&gt; so this is a coming soon!&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h3 id=&quot;customization-unique-to-the-verso-runtime&quot;&gt;Customization unique to the Verso runtime&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Tauri is largely made with the assumption of the underlying webview libraries, so there’re very little ways to use many Verso specific futures right now, for example, setting the verso executable path and resources directory are being done through global variables, which is not really applicable to window specific features (for example setting rounded corners), so we would like to add support for that next&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h2 id=&quot;thank-you&quot;&gt;Thank you&lt;/h2&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;At the end we want to thank &lt;a href=&quot;https://nlnet.nl/&quot;&gt;NLNet&lt;/a&gt; for making this project possible by supporting it financially through grants!&lt;/p&gt;
</content:encoded></item><item><title>Tauri 2.0 Stable Release</title><link>https://v2.tauri.app/blog/tauri-20/</link><guid isPermaLink="true">https://v2.tauri.app/blog/tauri-20/</guid><description>Tauri 2.0 stable is released!</description><pubDate>Wed, 02 Oct 2024 00:00:00 GMT</pubDate><content:encoded>&lt;p&gt;We are very proud to finally announce the stable release for the new major version of Tauri.
Welcome to Tauri 2.0!&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;svg width=&quot;48&quot; height=&quot;48&quot;&gt;&lt;use xlink:href=&quot;/assets/platforms.svg#wpf-android-os&quot;&gt;&lt;/use&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;svg width=&quot;48&quot; height=&quot;48&quot;&gt;&lt;use xlink:href=&quot;/assets/platforms.svg#simple-icons-ios&quot;&gt;&lt;/use&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;svg width=&quot;48&quot; height=&quot;48&quot;&gt;&lt;use xlink:href=&quot;/assets/platforms.svg#cib-linux&quot;&gt;&lt;/use&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;svg width=&quot;48&quot; height=&quot;48&quot;&gt;&lt;use xlink:href=&quot;/assets/platforms.svg#wpf-mac-os&quot;&gt;&lt;/use&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;svg width=&quot;48&quot; height=&quot;48&quot;&gt;&lt;use xlink:href=&quot;/assets/platforms.svg#bi-windows&quot;&gt;&lt;/use&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h2 id=&quot;what-is-tauri&quot;&gt;What is Tauri?&lt;/h2&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;aside aria-label=&quot;Definition&quot;&gt;&lt;p aria-hidden=&quot;true&quot;&gt;Definition&lt;/p&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;p&gt;Tauri is a framework for building tiny and fast binaries for all major desktop (macOS, linux, windows) and mobile (iOS, Android) platforms.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Developers can integrate any frontend framework that compiles to HTML, JavaScript, and CSS for building their user experience while leveraging languages such as Rust, Swift, and Kotlin for backend logic when needed.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/aside&gt;
&lt;p&gt;In a Tauri application the frontend is written in your favorite web frontend stack.
This runs inside the operating system WebView and communicates with the application core written mostly in Rust.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;&lt;img src=&quot;https://v2.tauri.app/_astro/tauri-trust-boundaries.C0cC9LAT_1KhzH4.svg&quot; alt=&quot;a graph showing the IPC bridge between the Application Core and the System’s WebView&quot; loading=&quot;lazy&quot; decoding=&quot;async&quot; width=&quot;1516&quot; height=&quot;753&quot;&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;aside aria-label=&quot;No Rust Skills Needed!&quot;&gt;&lt;p aria-hidden=&quot;true&quot;&gt;No Rust Skills Needed!&lt;/p&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;p&gt;You don’t need to write Code in Rust, Swift or Kotlin in most cases. Tauri already offers an extensive JavaScript API.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/aside&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h3 id=&quot;when-should-i-use-tauri&quot;&gt;When Should I Use Tauri?&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;If you check &lt;strong&gt;any&lt;/strong&gt; of the boxes below, you should use Tauri:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
&lt;li&gt;&lt;input type=&quot;checkbox&quot; disabled&gt; Do you want a single UI codebase for all platforms?&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;&lt;input type=&quot;checkbox&quot; disabled&gt; Do you want to reach as many users as possible on their platform (eg. Windows, MacOS, Linux, Android, iOS)?&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;&lt;input type=&quot;checkbox&quot; disabled&gt; Are you a frontend web developer and want to write native applications?&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;&lt;input type=&quot;checkbox&quot; disabled&gt; Are you a Rust developer looking to write applications with a nice looking UI with the option to do it in Rust?&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;&lt;input type=&quot;checkbox&quot; disabled&gt; Do you have an existing team of web developers and want to expand to native application markets with low upfront investment?&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;&lt;input type=&quot;checkbox&quot; disabled&gt; Do you have an existing team of rustaceans and want everything written in Rust?&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h2 id=&quot;how-popular-is-tauri&quot;&gt;How Popular is Tauri?&lt;/h2&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;&lt;img src=&quot;https://v2.tauri.app/_astro/tauri-star-history.Dr3CGlN3_5J4eO.webp&quot; alt=&quot;a graph showing the progression of Tauri GitHub stars over the years, starting with 0 at 2019 and continuing to grow past 80.000 in 2024&quot; loading=&quot;lazy&quot; decoding=&quot;async&quot; width=&quot;878&quot; height=&quot;579&quot;&gt;&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;On GitHub the Tauri repository has &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/pulls?q=is%3Apr+is%3Aclosed&quot;&gt;~4,878 Pull Requests&lt;/a&gt; and &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/issues?q=is%3Aissue+is%3Aclosed&quot;&gt;~3,570 Issues&lt;/a&gt; closed and around &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/discussions&quot;&gt;1000 discussions&lt;/a&gt;, at the time of writing.
To get a more detailed insight take a look at &lt;a href=&quot;https://ossinsight.io/analyze/tauri-apps/tauri&quot;&gt;the OSSinsight analysis&lt;/a&gt; of the Tauri repository.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Our &lt;a href=&quot;https://discord.gg/tauri&quot;&gt;Discord Server&lt;/a&gt; currently has ~17,700 members. We are seeing a lot of individual user support, questions on Tauri itself, questions directly to the working group or just discussions between fellow Tauri app developers.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;We are very happy about the positive and supportive community and grateful to all the community members answering or helping others in Discord or GitHub.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;We maintain a curated list of Tauri related projects, applications, plugins, guides and more at &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/awesome-tauri?tab=readme-ov-file#applications&quot;&gt;awesome-tauri&lt;/a&gt;. Check this out if you want to get inspiration, see what others are building and ideally create a PR to add your project.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Of course this is only a representative sample set and we don’t know exactly who else is building on Tauri.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h2 id=&quot;how-did-we-get-to-20&quot;&gt;How Did We Get to 2.0?&lt;/h2&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;In June 2022 we released Tauri 1.0 with a great impact on the desktop operating system market and how cross platform applications can be built.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;In the end of 2022 we released our initial alpha version of 2.0 to get initial feedback and to test out how mobile interaction should be defined.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;After the initial alpha we spent close to two years refining and changing the architecture of Tauri in public. After we saw the broad picture clear enough ourselves we released the beta in Februrary this year. At the same time we collaborated and worked with external security auditors to check our decisions, architecture changes and much more.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;This August we published the release candidate version of 2.0 to iron out major bugs and to get more feedback from productive use. At the same time the external audit was concluded and made public.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The release candidate time frame was considerably shorter and consisted mainly of high impact bugfixes and documentation improvements. Some breaking changes we had to make during the release candidate phase were bundled up until the end and are now included in the stable release. Take a look at the &lt;a href=&quot;#migration&quot;&gt;migration section&lt;/a&gt; if your main concern is upgrading from a previous version.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;In total we spent over two years working on improvements, new features, bugfixes, documentation, rewrites and a lot of discussions.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;This all happened while we released 8 minor versions of the Tauri 1.x branch and backported security fixes and other important bug fixes in several patch releases.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h2 id=&quot;who-made-this-release-possible&quot;&gt;Who Made This Release Possible?&lt;/h2&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;This release and Tauri itself is only possible due to massive amount of contributions from &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/lucasfernog&quot;&gt;Lucas&lt;/a&gt;, who has provided a constant stream of code changes over the years ❤️.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;&lt;img src=&quot;https://v2.tauri.app/_astro/lucas-commit-graph.gQ6-XyEM_HJIsi.webp&quot; alt=&quot;Lucas’ contribution graph, with 2744 commits, over 896.000 additions and 688.000 deletions.&quot; loading=&quot;lazy&quot; decoding=&quot;async&quot; width=&quot;644&quot; height=&quot;312&quot;&gt;&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Obviously, Lucas is not the only individual working and contributing to Tauri, but we feel he deserves a very special mention for carrying, starting, and supporting the project and its community throughout the years.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;We have had major contributions to the Tauri repository in 2.0 from &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/amrbashir&quot;&gt;Amr&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/FabianLars&quot;&gt;Fabian-Lars&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/Legend-Master&quot;&gt;Tony&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/chippers&quot;&gt;Chip&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/pewsheen&quot;&gt;Jason&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/wusyong&quot;&gt;YuWei&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/i-c-b&quot;&gt;icb&lt;/a&gt; , &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/simonhyll&quot;&gt;Simon&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/olivierlemasle&quot;&gt;Oliver Lemasle&lt;/a&gt; and many more contributors (&lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/graphs/contributors?from=9%2F24%2F2022&quot;&gt;source data&lt;/a&gt;).&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;We received an increasing number of drive-by contributors (one or very few PRs). We are grateful for these, but naming everyone would make this a very long list here.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;We have a lot(!) of &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/orgs/tauri-apps/repositories&quot;&gt;repositories&lt;/a&gt; in our organization, which are supporting the success of Tauri and without community and working group contributions Tauri would not be where it is now. A big thank you to everyone involved!&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Another special shout out and thanks for their constant involvement in the community goes to &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/FabianLars&quot;&gt;Fabian-Lars&lt;/a&gt; and &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/simonhyll&quot;&gt;Simon&lt;/a&gt;. If you have been involved in Tauri’s Discord or Github discussions you likely know their name or avatar.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;If you ever searched on Google or YouTube for Tauri, you have probably seen one of &lt;a href=&quot;https://www.youtube.com/c/JacobBolda&quot;&gt;Jacob’s streams&lt;/a&gt;. If that’s not the case please make sure to check it out and subscribe as his sessions are beyond just educational.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Another special place in our heart has the &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/governance-and-guidance/issues/58&quot;&gt;Tauri Board&lt;/a&gt;, highlighting &lt;a href=&quot;https://mt.linkedin.com/in/daniel-thompson-yvetot-a98301222&quot;&gt;Daniel Yvetot-Thompson&lt;/a&gt; for the numerous hours, sweat, blood and dedication to make Tauri known and sustainable.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;One important thing we should not forget, is that we acquired support from a stable partner of this open source project.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;&lt;img src=&quot;https://v2.tauri.app/_astro/crabnebula.xh9nhpEF_ZIM7Po.svg&quot; alt=&quot;CrabNebula Logo&quot; loading=&quot;lazy&quot; decoding=&quot;async&quot; width=&quot;1204&quot; height=&quot;210&quot;&gt;&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href=&quot;https://crabnebula.dev/&quot;&gt;CrabNebula&lt;/a&gt; granted multiple people mentioned above and others that are not mentioned here, the privilege to work on the Tauri ecosystem not only in their private time, but also during work time. You can find the &lt;a href=&quot;https://v2.tauri.app/blog/partnership-crabnebula/&quot;&gt;partnership announcement&lt;/a&gt; on our blog and we have been more than happy about this collaboration over the last year.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;In 2024 alone they spent over &lt;strong&gt;2,870&lt;/strong&gt; work hours on this project, which massively pushed the progress and allows us to announce the stable 2.0 release today.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;If you were not aware of CrabNebula yet, make sure to check out their &lt;a href=&quot;https://crabnebula.dev/&quot;&gt;products and services&lt;/a&gt; and consider the symbiotic relationship with Tauri if you are interested in not only improving your workflows, but also supporting the Tauri ecosystem.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h2 id=&quot;what-makes-20-great&quot;&gt;What Makes 2.0 Great?&lt;/h2&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;With this major release we improved and changed several aspects of how and where you can build, develop and publish your Tauri app. In the following sections we have more detailed insight. This does not cover everything, but should give you a decent impression on what you can expect from Tauri.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h3 id=&quot;getting-started-experience&quot;&gt;Getting Started Experience&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;One thing you are always going to go through when starting with a new framework or tool is the initial onboarding or getting started process.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;We value developer experience (DX) and try to make this initial process as seamless as building and distributing your final application.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;For this we created another project, which is called &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/create-tauri-app&quot;&gt;&lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;create-tauri-app&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/a&gt; or in short CTA.
This tool allows developers to start from scratch and get to a running Tauri app in a few minutes instead of hours.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;starlight-tabs data-sync-key=&quot;cmd&quot;&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;ul role=&quot;tablist&quot;&gt;&lt;li role=&quot;presentation&quot;&gt;&lt;a role=&quot;tab&quot; href=&quot;#tab-panel-4176&quot; id=&quot;tab-4176&quot; aria-selected=&quot;true&quot; tabindex=&quot;0&quot;&gt;Bash&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li role=&quot;presentation&quot;&gt;&lt;a role=&quot;tab&quot; href=&quot;#tab-panel-4177&quot; id=&quot;tab-4177&quot; aria-selected=&quot;false&quot; tabindex=&quot;-1&quot;&gt;PowerShell&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li role=&quot;presentation&quot;&gt;&lt;a role=&quot;tab&quot; href=&quot;#tab-panel-4178&quot; id=&quot;tab-4178&quot; aria-selected=&quot;false&quot; tabindex=&quot;-1&quot;&gt;Fish&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li role=&quot;presentation&quot;&gt;&lt;a role=&quot;tab&quot; href=&quot;#tab-panel-4179&quot; id=&quot;tab-4179&quot; aria-selected=&quot;false&quot; tabindex=&quot;-1&quot;&gt;npm&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li role=&quot;presentation&quot;&gt;&lt;a role=&quot;tab&quot; href=&quot;#tab-panel-4180&quot; id=&quot;tab-4180&quot; aria-selected=&quot;false&quot; tabindex=&quot;-1&quot;&gt;Yarn&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li role=&quot;presentation&quot;&gt;&lt;a role=&quot;tab&quot; href=&quot;#tab-panel-4181&quot; id=&quot;tab-4181&quot; aria-selected=&quot;false&quot; tabindex=&quot;-1&quot;&gt;pnpm&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li role=&quot;presentation&quot;&gt;&lt;a role=&quot;tab&quot; href=&quot;#tab-panel-4182&quot; id=&quot;tab-4182&quot; aria-selected=&quot;false&quot; tabindex=&quot;-1&quot;&gt;deno&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li role=&quot;presentation&quot;&gt;&lt;a role=&quot;tab&quot; href=&quot;#tab-panel-4183&quot; id=&quot;tab-4183&quot; aria-selected=&quot;false&quot; tabindex=&quot;-1&quot;&gt;bun&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li role=&quot;presentation&quot;&gt;&lt;a role=&quot;tab&quot; href=&quot;#tab-panel-4184&quot; id=&quot;tab-4184&quot; aria-selected=&quot;false&quot; tabindex=&quot;-1&quot;&gt;Cargo&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div id=&quot;tab-panel-4176&quot; aria-labelledby=&quot;tab-4176&quot; role=&quot;tabpanel&quot;&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;figure&gt;&lt;figcaption&gt;&lt;/figcaption&gt;&lt;pre&gt;&lt;code&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;sh&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;&amp;#x3C;(&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;&lt;span&gt;curl&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; https://create.tauri.app/sh&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;)&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/figure&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div id=&quot;tab-panel-4177&quot; aria-labelledby=&quot;tab-4177&quot; role=&quot;tabpanel&quot; hidden&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;figure&gt;&lt;figcaption&gt;&lt;/figcaption&gt;&lt;pre&gt;&lt;code&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;irm&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;https://create.tauri.app/ps&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;|&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;iex&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/figure&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div id=&quot;tab-panel-4178&quot; aria-labelledby=&quot;tab-4178&quot; role=&quot;tabpanel&quot; hidden&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;figure&gt;&lt;figcaption&gt;&lt;/figcaption&gt;&lt;pre&gt;&lt;code&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;sh&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;create&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;tauri-app&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/figure&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div id=&quot;tab-panel-4181&quot; aria-labelledby=&quot;tab-4181&quot; role=&quot;tabpanel&quot; hidden&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;figure&gt;&lt;figcaption&gt;&lt;/figcaption&gt;&lt;pre&gt;&lt;code&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;pnpm&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;create&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;tauri-app&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/figure&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div id=&quot;tab-panel-4182&quot; aria-labelledby=&quot;tab-4182&quot; role=&quot;tabpanel&quot; hidden&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;figure&gt;&lt;figcaption&gt;&lt;/figcaption&gt;&lt;pre&gt;&lt;code&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;deno&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;run&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;-A&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;npm:create-tauri-app&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/figure&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div id=&quot;tab-panel-4183&quot; aria-labelledby=&quot;tab-4183&quot; role=&quot;tabpanel&quot; hidden&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;figure&gt;&lt;figcaption&gt;&lt;/figcaption&gt;&lt;pre&gt;&lt;code&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;bun&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;create&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;tauri-app&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/figure&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div id=&quot;tab-panel-4184&quot; aria-labelledby=&quot;tab-4184&quot; role=&quot;tabpanel&quot; hidden&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;figure&gt;&lt;figcaption&gt;&lt;/figcaption&gt;&lt;pre&gt;&lt;code&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;cargo&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;install&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;create-tauri-app&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;--locked&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;cargo&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;create-tauri-app&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/figure&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;starlight-tabs-restore&gt;&lt;/starlight-tabs-restore&gt;&lt;/starlight-tabs&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Of course you need to install some prerequisites on your development system before you can start building your application. For this we have extensive guides with operating system specific sections in our &lt;a href=&quot;https://v2.tauri.app/start/prerequisites/&quot;&gt;official documentation&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;This whole onboarding experience has been improved and now also bootstraps mobile development templates for iOS and Android.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h3 id=&quot;hot-module-replacement&quot;&gt;Hot-Module Replacement&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;After the initial onboarding you will regularly develop and debug your Tauri application. We considered what would improve your development process already in 1.x of Tauri and extended the Hot-Module Replacement (HMR) to mobile devices and emulators.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;This means that all changes to the frontend of your application do not require a rebuild of your whole application and you can live preview how it will look like in the device or operating system your are developing for.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;video controls&gt;&lt;source src=&quot;/assets/hmr-preview.mp4&quot; type=&quot;video/mp4&quot;&gt;&lt;p&gt;Your browser does not support the video tag.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/video&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h3 id=&quot;plugins&quot;&gt;Plugins&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;With Tauri 2.0 we built a more advanced plugin system. We transferred a lot of our previous functionality into our official plugins (see &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/plugins-workspace&quot;&gt;plugins-workspace&lt;/a&gt;), to allow the community an easier entry into contributing to Tauri. We also hope to attract more maintainers for plugins and to speed up the process of implementing new features.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;This move to plugins has another benefit. We are going to be able to define a definition of done for Tauri’s core. We hope to stabilize the core functionality and offer a stable framework, where the moving parts are mostly plugins offering access to system specific functionality.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;You no longer need to understand all of Tauri to improve or implement specific features. The plugins usually do not depend on other plugins, with some exceptions. This means to implement a new file system access functionality it is only required to contribute to the &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;fs&lt;/code&gt; plugin instead of Tauri itself.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;As this release also targets mobile platforms, the plugin system also supports mobile plugins. You can write or re-use native code in Swift on iOS and Kotlin on Android and directly expose functions to the Tauri frontend using &lt;a href=&quot;https://kotlinlang.org/docs/annotations.html&quot;&gt;&lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;Annotations&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/a&gt; (&lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;@Command&lt;/code&gt; on Android), implementing a &lt;a href=&quot;https://docs.swift.org/swift-book/documentation/the-swift-programming-language/inheritance#Subclassing&quot;&gt;&lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;Subclass&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/a&gt; (&lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;YourPluginClass: Plugin&lt;/code&gt;) on iOS, or by invoking the Swift or Kotlin code from a Rust based Tauri command. Check out the &lt;a href=&quot;https://v2.tauri.app/develop/plugins/&quot;&gt;documentation&lt;/a&gt; on how to write your own plugin.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;As we are releasing Tauri as 2.0, the official plugins will follow the major version of Tauri to make compatibility with Tauri’s major version visible at a glance. Not all plugins are as stable as Tauri itself though.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Each plugin’s stableness is defined per plugin and documented (soon) in the plugin documentation. The plugin API can possibly break in minor versions, but we will try to keep these changes to a minimum, especially for plugins considered stable.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;aside aria-label=&quot;Tip&quot;&gt;&lt;p aria-hidden=&quot;true&quot;&gt;Tip&lt;/p&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;p&gt;You can pin your plugin versions to only patch updates if you need absolute stable interfaces. We generally try to backport security updates and will announce advisories on GitHub.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/aside&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;&lt;a href=&quot;https://v2.tauri.app/plugin/autostart/&quot;&gt;&lt;span&gt;Autostart&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/a&gt;&lt;p&gt;Automatically launch your app at system startup.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;div&gt;

      &lt;use xlink:href=&quot;/assets/platforms.svg#bi-windows&quot;&gt;&lt;/use&gt;
    &lt;/svg&gt;
  &lt;/div&gt;

  &lt;div&gt;
    &lt;svg width=&quot;24&quot; height=&quot;24&quot;&gt;
      &lt;use xlink:href=&quot;/assets/platforms.svg#cib-linux&quot;&gt;&lt;/use&gt;
    &lt;/svg&gt;
  &lt;/div&gt;

  &lt;div&gt;
    &lt;svg width=&quot;24&quot; height=&quot;24&quot;&gt;
      &lt;use xlink:href=&quot;/assets/platforms.svg#simple-icons-macos&quot;&gt;&lt;/use&gt;
    &lt;/svg&gt;
  &lt;/div&gt;

  &lt;div&gt;
    &lt;svg width=&quot;24&quot; height=&quot;24&quot;&gt;
      &lt;use xlink:href=&quot;/assets/platforms.svg#material-symbols-android&quot;&gt;&lt;/use&gt;
    &lt;/svg&gt;
  &lt;/div&gt;

  &lt;div&gt;
    &lt;svg width=&quot;24&quot; height=&quot;24&quot;&gt;
      &lt;use xlink:href=&quot;/assets/platforms.svg#simple-icons-ios&quot;&gt;&lt;/use&gt;
    &lt;/svg&gt;
  &lt;/div&gt;
&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;&lt;a href=&quot;https://v2.tauri.app/plugin/window-state/&quot;&gt;&lt;span&gt;Window State&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/a&gt;&lt;p&gt;Persist window sizes and positions.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;div&gt;
  &lt;div&gt;
    &lt;svg width=&quot;24&quot; height=&quot;24&quot;&gt;
      &lt;use xlink:href=&quot;/assets/platforms.svg#bi-windows&quot;&gt;&lt;/use&gt;
    &lt;/svg&gt;
  &lt;/div&gt;

  &lt;div&gt;
    &lt;svg width=&quot;24&quot; height=&quot;24&quot;&gt;
      &lt;use xlink:href=&quot;/assets/platforms.svg#cib-linux&quot;&gt;&lt;/use&gt;
    &lt;/svg&gt;
  &lt;/div&gt;

  &lt;div&gt;
    &lt;svg width=&quot;24&quot; height=&quot;24&quot;&gt;
      &lt;use xlink:href=&quot;/assets/platforms.svg#simple-icons-macos&quot;&gt;&lt;/use&gt;
    &lt;/svg&gt;
  &lt;/div&gt;
&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h3 id=&quot;mobile-support&quot;&gt;Mobile Support&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;A very much awaited part of this release is the mobile operating system support. The previous version of Tauri allowed to have a single UI code base for desktop operating systems but now this extends to iOS and Android.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;We have investigated and experimented with different solutions to support mobile and decided on using the operating system native language (Swift and Kotlin) to build an interface for the Rust code and to allow developers to write part of their functionality in these languages.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;This means you can re-use existing logic of your Swift or Kotlin app that interacts with the system and expose it to Rust or the frontend. Right now this works as mentioned above via the plugin system.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;We support development with an emulator or a real device and provide a lot of tooling to make the process as seamless as possible. We are not completely happy about the developer experience at the moment but are actively improving to bring it up to par with the desktop experience.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;On mobile not all of the official plugins are supported. Some are by design not a good fit for mobile and some are just not implemented to support mobile yet. If you would like to contribute on this part check the &lt;a href=&quot;#call-to-action&quot;&gt;last section&lt;/a&gt; of this post.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h3 id=&quot;the-allowlist-is-dead-long-live-the-allowlist&quot;&gt;The Allowlist is Dead, Long Live the Allowlist&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Yes, there is no &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;allowlist&lt;/code&gt; anymore, as we hit the limits of this system pretty quickly. We made it exclusive for Tauri core features and it did not even cover all of Tauri’s APIs. Our new system not only covers all of Tauri’s core API surface, it also supports app and plugin developers to implement their own access control and scoping with a unified approach.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The new system we implemented is using &lt;a href=&quot;https://v2.tauri.app/security/permissions/&quot;&gt;&lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;permissions&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/a&gt; - &lt;em&gt;“On-off toggles for Tauri commands”&lt;/em&gt;, &lt;a href=&quot;https://v2.tauri.app/security/scope/&quot;&gt;&lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;scopes&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/a&gt; - &lt;em&gt;“Parameter validation for Tauri commands”&lt;/em&gt; and &lt;a href=&quot;https://v2.tauri.app/security/capabilities/&quot;&gt;&lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;capabilities&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/a&gt; - &lt;em&gt;“Attaching permissions and scopes to Windows and WebViews”&lt;/em&gt;, to create a flexible but simple to use access control system.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;It allows the creation of named permission or scoping files and to re-use and combine them with other named permissions or scopes. This makes it possible to build more fine grained descriptive sets containing several simple or complex permissions and scopes.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;As a plugin developer you can abstract away several base permissions into a &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;default&lt;/code&gt; permission. This can be based on your default security assumptions and threat model. All official Tauri plugin default permissions are reasonably secure by default.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;As an app developer you can use, extend or reduce plugin permissions. Of course you can also build permissions and scopes for your own application.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;With this addition, Tauri’s core is now able to understand if a command invoke message from a frontend WebView is allowed to reach the command function. It is also able to attach the configured scope to the message.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The command implementation is responsible for interpreting and enforcing the scope. You can read more about our Threat Model and approach to security in our &lt;a href=&quot;https://v2.tauri.app/security/&quot;&gt;documentation&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h3 id=&quot;external-security-audit&quot;&gt;External Security Audit&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The major changes and architecture of v2 was independently audited by &lt;a href=&quot;https://www.radicallyopensecurity.com/&quot;&gt;Radically Open Security&lt;/a&gt; during the beta and release candidate period. Please take your time to read the &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/blob/dev/audits/Radically_Open_Security-v2-report.pdf&quot;&gt;report&lt;/a&gt; and learn more about the awesome work of &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/gronke&quot;&gt;@gronke&lt;/a&gt; and &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/pcwizz&quot;&gt;@pcwizz&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The whole audit was funded by the great folks at &lt;a href=&quot;https://nlnet.nl/&quot;&gt;NLNet&lt;/a&gt; via funding from &lt;a href=&quot;https://ngi.eu&quot;&gt;NGI&lt;/a&gt; and we are super grateful to be in the privileged position to get fully funded external security audits for major releases.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The results of this audit caused us to rewrite parts of how our dev server is exposed, specifically for mobile development. Without the help and guidance of the auditors this rewrite would not have been possible ❤️.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Additionally, we hardened our iFrame API exposure, fixed scope validation and resource identifier access for the &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;fs&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;http&lt;/code&gt; plugin, improved our inter-process communication stability, and many other security related fixes and improvements.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h3 id=&quot;inter-process-communication-ipc-rewrite&quot;&gt;Inter Process Communication (IPC) Rewrite&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;With the rewrite of our IPC layer we now support a long wished feature of Raw Payloads and generally changed how it works under the hood.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Previously &lt;strong&gt;all&lt;/strong&gt; IPC payloads were json serialized and deserialized which caused an overhead. This was noticeable once more than a few kilobytes were transfered between frontend and backend.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The new system supports &lt;a href=&quot;https://v2.tauri.app/develop/calling-rust/#accessing-raw-request&quot;&gt;Raw Requests&lt;/a&gt;. These speed up the transfer of large data from backend to frontend and vice versa, where you can either use raw bytes directly or use your own (de)serialization process (eg. bson, protobuf, avro and others).&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;For directly reading files from the filesystem into the WebView we still recommend the &lt;a href=&quot;https://v2.tauri.app/reference/javascript/api/namespacecore/#convertfilesrc&quot;&gt;&lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;convertFileSrc&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/a&gt; functionality, as it is most likely still faster if you do not need to process the data on the Rust backend.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h3 id=&quot;distribution-guides&quot;&gt;Distribution Guides&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;With Tauri 2.0 the distribution diversity greatly increased. Partially, due to the mobile ecosystem and partially due to our community contributions.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;We have official guides on how to ship to the &lt;a href=&quot;https://v2.tauri.app/distribute/app-store/&quot;&gt;Apple Appstore&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href=&quot;https://v2.tauri.app/distribute/google-play/&quot;&gt;Google Play&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href=&quot;https://v2.tauri.app/distribute/microsoft-store/&quot;&gt;Microsoft Store&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href=&quot;https://v2.tauri.app/distribute/crabnebula-cloud/&quot;&gt;CrabNebula Cloud&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href=&quot;https://v2.tauri.app/distribute/flatpak/&quot;&gt;Flathub&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href=&quot;https://v2.tauri.app/distribute/snapcraft/&quot;&gt;Snapcraft&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href=&quot;https://v2.tauri.app/distribute/aur/&quot;&gt;AUR&lt;/a&gt; and more distribution formats in our &lt;a href=&quot;https://v2.tauri.app/distribute/&quot;&gt;distribution docs&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;aside aria-label=&quot;Github Action&quot;&gt;&lt;p aria-hidden=&quot;true&quot;&gt;Github Action&lt;/p&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;p&gt;Our GitHub action (&lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri-action&lt;/code&gt;) is in progress to support automated building for the mobile operating systems but does not support it yet.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/aside&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h2 id=&quot;changelog&quot;&gt;Changelog&lt;/h2&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;This section contains all changes going from 1.x in a concise list.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;details&gt;&lt;summary&gt;&lt;i&gt;Show the Full List&lt;/i&gt;&lt;/summary&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;h3 id=&quot;added&quot;&gt;Added&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;ul&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added Mobile support.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added multiwebview support behind the unstable feature flag. See WindowBuilder and WebviewBuilder for more information.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;rustls-tls&lt;/code&gt; cargo feature flag&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;shadow&lt;/code&gt; option when creating a webview window,&lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;WebviewWindow::set_shadow&lt;/code&gt; method in Rust and equivalent API in JS.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::Webview&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::WebviewBuilder&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::WebviewWindow&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::WebviewWindowBuilder&lt;/code&gt; structs in Rust and equivalent classes in Js. The old &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::Window&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::WindowBuilder&lt;/code&gt; behaviors have moved to &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::WebviewWindow&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::WebviewWindowBuilder&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::scope::fs&lt;/code&gt; module&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::App/AppHandle::default_window_icon&lt;/code&gt; method.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::ipc&lt;/code&gt; module with IPC primitives.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::ipc::Channel&lt;/code&gt; type and equivalent JS &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;Channel&lt;/code&gt; type to send data across the IPC.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;incognito&lt;/code&gt; option when creating a webview window.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;windowEffects&lt;/code&gt; option when creating a webview window and &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;WebviewWindow::set_effects&lt;/code&gt; to try and change effects at runtime.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::path::PathResolver&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::Manager::path&lt;/code&gt; method to access the new &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;PathResolver&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;visibleOnAllWorkspaces&lt;/code&gt; option when creating a webview window.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::App/AppHandle::primary_monitor&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;App/AppHandle::available_monitors&lt;/code&gt; methods.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::plugin::Builder::on_navigation&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::plugin::Plugin::on_navigation&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::WebviewWindow::navigate&lt;/code&gt; method&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::RunEvent::Opened&lt;/code&gt; on macOS and iOS for deep link support.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added file associations support in bundler.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::App/AppHandle::cleanup_before_exit&lt;/code&gt; to manually call the cleanup logic. You should always exit the tauri app immediately after this function returns and not use any tauri-related APIs.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;On Linux, add &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::WebviewWindow::default_vbox&lt;/code&gt; method to get a reference to the &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;gtk::Box&lt;/code&gt; that contains the menu bar and the webview.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;linux-libxdo&lt;/code&gt; cargo feature flag (disabled by default) to enable linking to &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;libxdo&lt;/code&gt; which is used to make &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;Cut&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;Copy&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;Paste&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;SelectAll&lt;/code&gt; native menu items work on Linux.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;On macOS, add &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::WebviewWindow::ns_view&lt;/code&gt; method to get a pointer to the &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;NSWindow&lt;/code&gt; content view.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::Builder::register_asynchronous_uri_scheme_protocol&lt;/code&gt; to allow resolving a custom URI scheme protocol request asynchronously to prevent blocking the main thread.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Included drop and hover position for drag and drop events.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::WebviewWindow::set_progress_bar&lt;/code&gt; method&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::WebviewWindow::set_always_on_bottom&lt;/code&gt; method and &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;alwaysOnTop&lt;/code&gt; option when creating a webview window.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::WebviewWindowBuilder::on_page_load&lt;/code&gt; method.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;common-controls-v6&lt;/code&gt; cargo feature flag (enabled by default).&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;Window::destroy&lt;/code&gt; to force close a window.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::EventId&lt;/code&gt; type&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::WindowBuilder::on_download&lt;/code&gt; to handle download request events.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::WebviewWindowBuilder::parent&lt;/code&gt; which is a convenient wrapper around parent functionality for Windows, Linux and macOS.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::WebviewWindowBuilder::owner&lt;/code&gt; on Windows only.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::WebviewWindowBuilder::transient_for&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::WebviewWindowBuilder::transient_for_raw&lt;/code&gt; on Linux only.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::WebviewWindow::start_resize_dragging&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::ResizeDirection&lt;/code&gt; enum.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::WebviewWindowBuilder::proxy_url&lt;/code&gt; method.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::WebviewEvent&lt;/code&gt; enum&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::RunEvent::WebviewEvent&lt;/code&gt; variant.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::Builder::on_webview_event&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::Webview::on_webview_event&lt;/code&gt; methods.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::image&lt;/code&gt; module which includes&lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::image::Image&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::image::JsImage&lt;/code&gt; types and &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::image::include_img!&lt;/code&gt; macro.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::is_dev&lt;/code&gt; function to determine whether the app is running in development mode or not.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::Assets::setup&lt;/code&gt; method on &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::Assets&lt;/code&gt; trait that lets you run initialization code for your custom asset provider.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::Rect&lt;/code&gt; struct.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::WebviewWindow::set_zoom&lt;/code&gt; method&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;zoomHotkeys&lt;/code&gt; option when creating a webview window.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;window.isTauri&lt;/code&gt; JS global function to check whether running in tauri or not.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;specta&lt;/code&gt; feature flag which adds &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;specta&lt;/code&gt; support for &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;AppHandle&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;State&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;Window&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;Webview&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;WebviewWindow&lt;/code&gt; types.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::App/AppHandle/WebviewWindow::cursor_position&lt;/code&gt; getter to get the current cursor position.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::App/AppHandle/WebviewWindow::monitor_from_point(x,y)&lt;/code&gt; getter to get the monitor from a given point..&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::RunEvent::Reopen&lt;/code&gt; to handle click on dock icon on macOS.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;defaultWindowIcon&lt;/code&gt; to the JS &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;app&lt;/code&gt; module to retrieve the default window icon in JS.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::WebviewWindow::set_title_bar_style&lt;/code&gt; to set title bar at runtime on macOS.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Add APIs to enable setting window size constraints separately:
&lt;ul&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::WindowBuilder::inner_size_constraints&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::WebviewWindowBuilder::inner_size_constraints&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::WindowSizeConstraints&lt;/code&gt; struct&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::Window::set_size_constraints&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::WebviewWindow::set_size_constraints&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;h3 id=&quot;enhancements&quot;&gt;Enhancements&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;ul&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Use custom protocols on the IPC implementation to enhance performance.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Enhance centering a newly created window, it will no longer jump to center after being visible.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;The &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;custom-protocol&lt;/code&gt; Cargo feature is no longer required on your application and is now ignored. To check if running on production, use &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;#[cfg(not(dev))]&lt;/code&gt; instead of &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;#[cfg(feature = &quot;custom-protocol&quot;)]&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Improved the JS &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;path&lt;/code&gt; APIs to return simplified paths on Windows when possible, i.e removing UNC (&lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;\\?\&lt;/code&gt;) prefix.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Improved the error message that is shown when deserializing the Tauri plugin config.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Set the gtk application id to the &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;identifier&lt;/code&gt; defined in &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri.conf.json&lt;/code&gt; to ensure the app uniqueness. This can be disabled by setting &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;enableGtkAppId&lt;/code&gt; option to &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;false&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;On Windows, handle resizing undecorated windows natively which improves performance and fixes a couple of annoyances with previous JS implementation:
&lt;ul&gt;
&lt;li&gt;No more cursor flickering when moving the cursor across an edge.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Can resize from top even when &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;data-tauri-drag-region&lt;/code&gt; element exists there.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Upon starting rezing, clicks don’t go through elements behind it so no more accidental clicks.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Mark &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;AppHandle::restart&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;process::restart&lt;/code&gt; as &lt;a href=&quot;https://doc.rust-lang.org/rust-by-example/fn/diverging.html&quot;&gt;diverging functions&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;h3 id=&quot;bug-fixes&quot;&gt;Bug Fixes&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;ul&gt;
&lt;li&gt;No longer unpacking and flattening the &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;payload&lt;/code&gt; over the IPC so that commands with arguments called &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;cmd&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;callback&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;error&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;options&lt;/code&gt; or &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;payload&lt;/code&gt; aren’t breaking the IPC.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Fix calling &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;set_activation_policy&lt;/code&gt; when the event loop is running.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Fix can not prevent closing a window from another webview.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;On Windows, fix decorated window not transparent initially until resized.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Resolve symlinks on the filesystem scope check.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Fix the JS &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;basename(path, &apos;ext&apos;)&lt;/code&gt; API implementation removing all occurances of &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;ext&lt;/code&gt; where it should only remove the last one.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Fix window white flashing on exit on Windows&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Apply &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;minWidth&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;minHieght&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;maxWidth&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;maxHeight&lt;/code&gt; constraints separately, which fixes a long standing bug where these constraints were never applied unless width and height were constrained together.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;h3 id=&quot;changed&quot;&gt;Changed&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;ul&gt;
&lt;li&gt;The window creation and setup hook are now called when the event loop is ready.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Renamed the &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;default-tls&lt;/code&gt; feature to &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;native-tls&lt;/code&gt; and.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Changed the plugin setup hook to take a second argument of type &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;PluginApi&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Changed &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::Window&lt;/code&gt; struct behavior and moved its old behavior to the new &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::WebviewWindow&lt;/code&gt; type.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Moved &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::api::path&lt;/code&gt; module to &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::path&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Moved all functions from &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::api::path&lt;/code&gt; to be methods on &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::path::PathResolver&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Renamed &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;system-tray&lt;/code&gt; feature flag to &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tray-icon&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Changed &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::App::handle&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::Manager::app_handle&lt;/code&gt; methods to return a reference to an &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;AppHandle&lt;/code&gt; instead of an owned value.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Changed &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::Builder::register_uri_scheme_protocol&lt;/code&gt; to return a &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;http::Response&lt;/code&gt; instead of &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;Result&amp;#x3C;http::Response&gt;&lt;/code&gt;. To return an error response, manually create a response with status code &gt;= 400.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;The custom protocol on Windows and Android now uses the &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;http&lt;/code&gt; scheme instead of &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;https&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Changed &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::Env.args&lt;/code&gt; to &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::Env.args_os&lt;/code&gt; and now uses &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;OsString&lt;/code&gt; instead of &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;String&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Changed &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;TAURI_AUTOMATION&lt;/code&gt; env var to &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;TAURI_WEBVIEW_AUTOMATION&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Changed &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::Builder::invoke_system&lt;/code&gt; to take references instead of owned values.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Changed&lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::Builder::invoke_system&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::Builder::on_page_load&lt;/code&gt; hooks to take a &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::Webview&lt;/code&gt; argument instead of a &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::Window&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Moved the &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::command&lt;/code&gt; module items to the &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::ipc&lt;/code&gt; module so its import name does not clash with the &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::command&lt;/code&gt; macro.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Changed &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::App::run_iteration&lt;/code&gt; to take a callback and removed its return value.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Changed &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;AppHandle::exit&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;AppHandle::restart&lt;/code&gt; to trigger &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;RunEvent::ExitRequested&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;RunEvent::Exit&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Renamed &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::WebviewWindowBuilder::owner_window&lt;/code&gt; to &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::WebviewWindowBuilder::owner_raw&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::WebviewWindowBuilder::parent_window&lt;/code&gt; to &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::WebviewWindowBuilder::parent_raw&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Renamed the &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;window-data-url&lt;/code&gt; feature flag to &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;webview-data-url&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Changed &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::WebviewWindow::close&lt;/code&gt; to trigger a close requested event instead of forcing the window to be closed. Use &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::WebviewWindow::destroy&lt;/code&gt; to force close.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Renamed &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;icon-ico&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;icon-png&lt;/code&gt; feature flags to &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;image-ico&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;image-png&lt;/code&gt; respectively.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Removed &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::Icon&lt;/code&gt; enum, use the new &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::Image&lt;/code&gt; type instead. All APIs that previously accepted &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::Icon&lt;/code&gt; have changed to accept &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::Image&lt;/code&gt; instead.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Changed &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::Context&lt;/code&gt; struct and &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::Assets&lt;/code&gt; trait to have a &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;R: Runtime&lt;/code&gt; generic.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Renamed &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::Context::assets_mut&lt;/code&gt; to &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::Context::set_assets&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Changed &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::Context&lt;/code&gt; type to not have &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;&amp;#x3C;A: Assets&gt;&lt;/code&gt; generic so the assets implementation can be swapped with &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;Context::set_assets&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Changed &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::Context::assets&lt;/code&gt; to return &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;&amp;#x26;dyn Assets&lt;/code&gt; instead of &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;&amp;#x26;A&lt;/code&gt; generic.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Renamed &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::FileDropEvent&lt;/code&gt; enum to &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::DragDropEvent&lt;/code&gt; and renamed its variants. Also renamed the js events&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Renamed &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::WindowEvent::FileDrop&lt;/code&gt; enum variant to &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::WindowEvent::DragDrop&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
