&lt;p&gt;One of the most &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/issues/4338&quot;&gt;requested features&lt;/a&gt; is native context menus. It is finally available with both Rust and JavaScript APIs powered by &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/muda&quot;&gt;muda&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h3 id=&quot;window-apis&quot;&gt;Window APIs&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Several new window APIs have been implemented, making your app much more configurable.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h3 id=&quot;mobile-apis&quot;&gt;Mobile APIs&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The v2 release comes with some mobile native API support by default. Currently there is support for notifications, dialogs, NFC, barcode reading, biometric authentication, clipboard and deep link. More APIs will be added soon after the stable release.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h2 id=&quot;audit&quot;&gt;Audit&lt;/h2&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;We are currently being audited to ensure v2 is safe, similar to what we did for the v1 stable release.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h2 id=&quot;stability&quot;&gt;Stability&lt;/h2&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The API is not stable yet, but no major changes are expected. As soon as the audit is completed and the changes are done, we will promote to a Release Candidate and a v2 stable release soon after that. Stay tuned!&lt;/p&gt;
</content:encoded></item><item><title>Strengthening Tauri: Our Partnership with CrabNebula</title><link>https://v2.tauri.app/blog/partnership-crabnebula/</link><guid isPermaLink="true">https://v2.tauri.app/blog/partnership-crabnebula/</guid><description>As an open-source project, Tauri&apos;s primary mission has always been to provide a secure, efficient framework for multi-platform application development. Understanding the concerns and needs of our community, we are excited to share insights into our partnership with CrabNebula and how it bolsters the stability and future of Tauri.</description><pubDate>Mon, 13 Nov 2023 00:00:00 GMT</pubDate><content:encoded>&lt;p&gt;&lt;img alt=&quot;Hero Image&quot; loading=&quot;lazy&quot; decoding=&quot;async&quot; width=&quot;1280&quot; height=&quot;640&quot; src=&quot;https://v2.tauri.app/_astro/header.DZMzZltK_ZR9ea2.webp&quot; srcset=&quot;&quot;&gt;&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;As an open-source project, Tauri’s primary mission has always been to provide a secure, efficient framework for multi-platform application development. Understanding the concerns and needs of our community, we are excited to share insights into our partnership with CrabNebula and how it bolsters the stability and future of Tauri.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h2 id=&quot;crabnebulas-contribution-to-stability-and-security&quot;&gt;CrabNebula’s Contribution to Stability and Security&lt;/h2&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;CrabNebula’s role in Tauri’s journey over the past year has been pivotal. By dedicating full-time engineers to work explicitly on Tauri’s development and maintenance, they are helping us enhance the framework’s stability and security. They are literally being paid to resolve bugs and issues, pushing forward on the 2.0 release, and offering their support in the many Discord discussions.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Specifically, we would like to call out some folks from CrabNebula for helping drive the community and the code forward: Daniel, Lucas, Elizabeth, Yu-Wei, Tillmann, Amr, Chip, Fabian-Lars, Alve, Atila, Eleftheria, Tejas, Lorenzo, Robin, David, Jason, and Te-Kai.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Their efforts in conducting thorough security audits of minor releases are particularly crucial. These audits not only help us identify and fix vulnerabilities but also reinforce our commitment to delivering a secure framework for all users. Collaborative features developed with Impierce and Kino AI under this partnership further expand Tauri’s capabilities, which will help it to remain cutting-edge and reliable.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h2 id=&quot;a-partnership-model-focused-on-open-source-values&quot;&gt;A Partnership Model Focused on Open Source Values&lt;/h2&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Our collaboration with CrabNebula is grounded in open-source principles. It’s designed not just for mutual benefit but to serve as a sustainable model for future collaborations. We understand the importance of maintaining the integrity and independence of Tauri as an open-source project. This partnership respects these values, ensuring that Tauri continues to be driven by community needs and open-source innovation.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h2 id=&quot;long-term-impact-on-the-tauri-ecosystem&quot;&gt;Long-Term Impact on the Tauri Ecosystem&lt;/h2&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The partnership is more than just a short-term alliance; it’s a strategic move to secure the long-term future of Tauri. By bringing additional resources and diverse perspectives, we’re enhancing the framework’s robustness. Importantly, involvement of companies using Tauri, facilitated by this partnership, gets cycled directly into the development process. This feedback loop is vital for addressing real-world usage challenges, because it is important to all of us that Tauri remains relevant and continues to evolve according to the needs of its users.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h2 id=&quot;creating-channels-for-community-engagement&quot;&gt;Creating Channels for Community Engagement&lt;/h2&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;In line with our commitment to community engagement, we will be launching a #crabnebula channel on Tauri’s Discord. This will be a dedicated space for direct communication, collaboration, and feedback. We want to make sure that our community’s voice is heard and integrated into the ongoing development of Tauri.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h2 id=&quot;call-to-action&quot;&gt;Call to Action&lt;/h2&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;To our developers, contributors, users, and donors - we invite you to engage with us on this journey. Your input is invaluable as we continue to develop Tauri in partnership with CrabNebula. Together, we are not just building a framework; we are seeking ways to enhance its longevity and relevance in the ever-changing world of technology.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;If you like, go over to the CrabNebula blog and read &lt;a href=&quot;https://crabnebula.dev/blog/tauri-partnership&quot;&gt;their perspective&lt;/a&gt; on this partnership.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
&lt;li&gt;&lt;a href=&quot;https://discord.gg/tauri&quot;&gt;Discord Tauri&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;&lt;a href=&quot;https://x.com/TauriApps&quot;&gt;X Tauri&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;&lt;a href=&quot;https://fosstodon.org/@TauriApps&quot;&gt;Mastadon Tauri&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;&lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps&quot;&gt;GitHub Tauri&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;&lt;a href=&quot;https://tauri.app&quot;&gt;Website Tauri&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;&lt;a href=&quot;https://x.com/CrabNebulaDev&quot;&gt;X CrabNebula&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;&lt;a href=&quot;https://fosstodon.org/@crabnebula&quot;&gt;Mastadon CrabNebula&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;&lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/crabnebula-dev&quot;&gt;GitHub CrabNebula&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;&lt;a href=&quot;https://crabnebula.dev&quot;&gt;Website Crabnebula&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
</content:encoded></item><item><title>Announcing Tauri 1.5.0</title><link>https://v2.tauri.app/blog/tauri-1-5/</link><guid isPermaLink="true">https://v2.tauri.app/blog/tauri-1-5/</guid><description>The Tauri team is happy to announce the 1.5.0 release.</description><pubDate>Thu, 28 Sep 2023 00:00:00 GMT</pubDate><content:encoded>&lt;p&gt;&lt;img src=&quot;https://v2.tauri.app/_astro/header.DdVdFRx8_17pIhA.webp&quot; alt=&quot;Tauri 1.5 Launch Hero Image&quot; loading=&quot;lazy&quot; decoding=&quot;async&quot; width=&quot;1280&quot; height=&quot;640&quot;&gt;&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The Tauri team is excited to announce the 1.5 release. This version includes several new features and important bug fixes such as improved resources bundling, code signing enhancements, &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;notarytool&lt;/code&gt; migration on macOS and Bun support.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h2 id=&quot;upgrading&quot;&gt;Upgrading&lt;/h2&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Make sure to update both NPM and Cargo dependencies to the 1.5.0 release. You can update the dependencies with:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;starlight-tabs data-sync-key=&quot;cmd&quot;&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;ul role=&quot;tablist&quot;&gt;&lt;li role=&quot;presentation&quot;&gt;&lt;a role=&quot;tab&quot; href=&quot;#tab-panel-4201&quot; id=&quot;tab-4201&quot; aria-selected=&quot;true&quot; tabindex=&quot;0&quot;&gt;npm&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li role=&quot;presentation&quot;&gt;&lt;a role=&quot;tab&quot; href=&quot;#tab-panel-4202&quot; id=&quot;tab-4202&quot; aria-selected=&quot;false&quot; tabindex=&quot;-1&quot;&gt;yarn&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li role=&quot;presentation&quot;&gt;&lt;a role=&quot;tab&quot; href=&quot;#tab-panel-4203&quot; id=&quot;tab-4203&quot; aria-selected=&quot;false&quot; tabindex=&quot;-1&quot;&gt;pnpm&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li role=&quot;presentation&quot;&gt;&lt;a role=&quot;tab&quot; href=&quot;#tab-panel-4204&quot; id=&quot;tab-4204&quot; aria-selected=&quot;false&quot; tabindex=&quot;-1&quot;&gt;cargo&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div id=&quot;tab-panel-4201&quot; aria-labelledby=&quot;tab-4201&quot; role=&quot;tabpanel&quot;&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;figure&gt;&lt;figcaption&gt;&lt;/figcaption&gt;&lt;pre&gt;&lt;code&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;npm&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;install&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;@tauri-apps/cli@latest&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;@tauri-apps/api@latest&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/figure&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div id=&quot;tab-panel-4202&quot; aria-labelledby=&quot;tab-4202&quot; role=&quot;tabpanel&quot; hidden&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;figure&gt;&lt;figcaption&gt;&lt;/figcaption&gt;&lt;pre&gt;&lt;code&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;yarn&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;upgrade&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;@tauri-apps/cli&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;@tauri-apps/api&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;--latest&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/figure&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div id=&quot;tab-panel-4203&quot; aria-labelledby=&quot;tab-4203&quot; role=&quot;tabpanel&quot; hidden&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;figure&gt;&lt;figcaption&gt;&lt;/figcaption&gt;&lt;pre&gt;&lt;code&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;pnpm&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;update&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;@tauri-apps/cli&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;@tauri-apps/api&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;--latest&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/figure&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div id=&quot;tab-panel-4204&quot; aria-labelledby=&quot;tab-4204&quot; role=&quot;tabpanel&quot; hidden&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;figure&gt;&lt;figcaption&gt;&lt;/figcaption&gt;&lt;pre&gt;&lt;code&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;cargo&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;update&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/figure&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;starlight-tabs-restore&gt;&lt;/starlight-tabs-restore&gt;&lt;/starlight-tabs&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h2 id=&quot;whats-in-150&quot;&gt;What’s in 1.5.0&lt;/h2&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h3 id=&quot;notarytool&quot;&gt;Notarytool&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;At WWDC 2021 Apple introduced notarytool, a new tool for interacting with the Apple notary service.
Before the 1.5 release, Tauri used altool to notarize your application, but that tool has been deprecated and will stop working for notarization on 2023-11-01.
You &lt;strong&gt;must&lt;/strong&gt; upgrade your Tauri CLI to 1.5 before that.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;If you are using API keys for authentication with the notary service, notarytool no longer automatically searches for your APi key &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;.p8&lt;/code&gt; file.
We recommend users to define its path via the &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;APPLE_API_KEY_PATH&lt;/code&gt; environment variable, though to avoid breaking changes we perform the same lookup done by altool to find your key file in case you did not set the environment variable.
In the future, this might change, so please adjust your publish pipelines accordingly.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h3 id=&quot;bun-support&quot;&gt;Bun support&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The Tauri CLI now supports the &lt;a href=&quot;https://bun.sh/&quot;&gt;Bun&lt;/a&gt; package manager.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;We would like to thank &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/colinhacks&quot;&gt;@colinhacks&lt;/a&gt; for submitting the pull requests for this feature!&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h3 id=&quot;code-signing-improvements&quot;&gt;Code signing improvements&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Starting on Tauri 1.5, our bundler now signs all executables (including sidecars, app executables and NSIS uninstaller) and macOS frameworks.
We also improved our notarization algorithm adding support to the &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;APPLE_TEAM_ID&lt;/code&gt; environment variable to properly define the team ID associated with your account in case you belong to multiple teams.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;We would like to thank &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tr3ysmith&quot;&gt;@tr3ysmith&lt;/a&gt; for submitting the pull requests for this feature!&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h3 id=&quot;macos-frameworks&quot;&gt;macOS frameworks&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;This release comes with enhanced macOS frameworks support by code signing all custom frameworks you inject via &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri.conf.json &gt; tauri &gt; bundle &gt; macOS &gt; frameworks&lt;/code&gt; and defining the &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;@rpath&lt;/code&gt; value fixing a crash when updating your app.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;We would like to thank &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tr3ysmith&quot;&gt;@tr3ysmith&lt;/a&gt; for submitting the pull requests for this feature!&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h3 id=&quot;mixed-content-on-windows&quot;&gt;Mixed content on Windows&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;We now offer a configuration option to switch our custom protocol on Windows to use the &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;http&lt;/code&gt; scheme instead of &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;https&lt;/code&gt;.
This reduces the security of your application on Windows since it allows connecting to insecure endpoints such as &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;ws://url&lt;/code&gt;,
but it matches the behavior on Linux and macOS custom protocols. To enable it, set the &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri.conf.json &gt; tauri &gt; security &gt; dangerousUseHttpScheme&lt;/code&gt; to true.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h3 id=&quot;other-changes&quot;&gt;Other changes&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Check out the entire list of changes:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
&lt;li&gt;&lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/releases/tag/tauri-v1.5.0&quot;&gt;tauri&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;&lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/releases/tag/tauri-cli-v1.5.0&quot;&gt;tauri-cli&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;&lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/releases/tag/tauri-bundler-v1.4.0&quot;&gt;tauri-bundler&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;</content:encoded></item><item><title>Roadmap to Tauri 2.0</title><link>https://v2.tauri.app/blog/roadmap-to-tauri-2-0/</link><guid isPermaLink="true">https://v2.tauri.app/blog/roadmap-to-tauri-2-0/</guid><description>Tauri 1.0 was released in June 2022 and introduced an app toolkit for developers to build apps using HTML, CSS, and JavaScript with the security and performance of Rust.</description><pubDate>Thu, 07 Sep 2023 00:00:00 GMT</pubDate><content:encoded>&lt;p&gt;&lt;img alt=&quot;Hero Image&quot; loading=&quot;lazy&quot; decoding=&quot;async&quot; width=&quot;1280&quot; height=&quot;640&quot; src=&quot;https://v2.tauri.app/_astro/header.CWI_0uF__1GYYfu.webp&quot; srcset=&quot;&quot;&gt;&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Tauri 1.0 was &lt;a href=&quot;https://v2.tauri.app/blog/tauri-1-0/&quot;&gt;released in June 2022&lt;/a&gt; and introduced an app toolkit for developers to build apps using HTML, CSS, and JavaScript with the security and performance of Rust. Tauri has been pivotal in redefining and asking the question: “What is an app?”&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Version 1.0 launched with support for Linux, macOS, and Windows and has been updated with new features, DX improvements, and bug fixes to bring us to &lt;a href=&quot;https://v2.tauri.app/blog/tauri-1-4/&quot;&gt;Tauri 1.4&lt;/a&gt; that is available now.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;But there’s one question the Tauri Working Group gets asked time and time again. We affectionately refer to it as “Mobile when?!?” and today we’d like to outline the path ahead of us to get to Tauri 2.0 and to answer that question.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h2 id=&quot;what-is-tauri-20&quot;&gt;What is Tauri 2.0?&lt;/h2&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;In essence, the 2.0 release of Tauri is The Mobile Update. But 2.0 is so much more than just mobile. Here are a few of the features that will be included:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
&lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Powerful Plugins&lt;/strong&gt;: Many of the Tauri APIs have been shifted to use the Tauri plugin system. This allows us to make Tauri code more modular, more maintainable, but also enables us to make the plugin system more powerful for developers to build their own plugins.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Swift and Kotlin Bindings for Plugins&lt;/strong&gt;: Now you can write platform-specific code in Swift and Kotlin. Tauri has offered a bridge between Rust and JavaScript code since version 1.0. With Tauri 2.0, plugin developers will be able to write code in Swift and Kotlin to integrate more closely with the systems they’re developing for.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Support for iOS and Android&lt;/strong&gt;: You’ll be able to build Tauri apps and run them on iOS and Android.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;!-- TODO: Did I phrase all of the above correctly? Anything else that we want added in? --&gt;
&lt;p&gt;We’ve already seen developers doing amazing things with the prerelease version of Tauri 2.0 and we’re excited to move forward to a stable version to get it in the hands of everyone.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h2 id=&quot;path-to-tauri-20-stable&quot;&gt;Path to Tauri 2.0 Stable&lt;/h2&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Right now, we are in the 2.0 alpha phase. Here are the 3 key milestones to get us to Tauri 2.0 stable:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ol&gt;
&lt;li&gt;&lt;a href=&quot;#beta&quot;&gt;Beta&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;&lt;a href=&quot;#release-candidate&quot;&gt;Release Candidate&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;&lt;a href=&quot;#stable&quot;&gt;Stable&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ol&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Below are the steps within each of those milestones that the Tauri Working Group are driving towards.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h3 id=&quot;beta&quot;&gt;Beta&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;To enter a beta phase Tauri 2.0 must be feature-complete and working with no known major issues. This means that the Tauri Working Group are satisfied with the public Tauri APIs and don’t anticipate any breaking changes (although they are possible as we receive community feedback).&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Once we’ve entered beta then we will lock down the code base and move onto an auditing phase. No new features will be targeted for Tauri 2.0 after this point.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Security is very important to Tauri. We work with external auditors to review Tauri code so that it can be as secure as possible for Tauri developers and their users. A similar approach was taken with Tauri 1.0 in collaboration with Radically Open Security (&lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/blob/dev/audits/Radically_Open_Security-v1-report.pdf&quot;&gt;Tauri 1.0 Security Audit Report&lt;/a&gt;).&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;More details on the security audit for 2.0 are to come in the future.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h3 id=&quot;release-candidate&quot;&gt;Release Candidate&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Once all of the audit findings are resolved then we will move on to the release candidate phase. This is where we’ll ask the Tauri community to give it a test drive, see if there are any bugs, and to provide feedback in preparation for the stable release.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The RC phase will be time-locked so that early adopters can help us discover pain points and low-hanging fruit that can be resolved quickly. This will also include a documentation sprint in order to align what we know with what we show. More details on this will be shared as we get get closer to the release candidate phase.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h3 id=&quot;stable&quot;&gt;Stable&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Tauri 2.0 will be released and generally available for everyone to build amazing things with. We will also continue fixing bugs and releasing updates in line with our current approach of publishing patches and minor updates.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h3 id=&quot;where-we-are&quot;&gt;Where We Are&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;There are a lot of moving parts in moving towards a stable release. The two main parts are the findings and fixes from the security audit alongside the feedback and adjustments from the community. These two pieces are incredibly important and we want to be sure we can prioritize those without sacrificing the security and quality of Tauri.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Because of those priorities we don’t yet have hard timelines for a Tauri 2.0 stable release. We have internal targets that we’re aiming for to keep us on track, but we want to be sure that we’re flexible to accommodate feedback.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;What we can share right now is that we’re roughly targeting the stable release of Tauri 2.0 in early 2024. We’re driving sharply ahead to enter the beta phase as soon as possible.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;As we make progress towards Tauri 2.0 we’ll be sure to share updates to the community. The primary way to keep up to date is to watch for the Tauri releases as they move through the beta, release candidate, and lastly stable phases. You can also keep an eye on &lt;a href=&quot;https://twitter.com/TauriApps&quot;&gt;Twitter&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href=&quot;https://fosstodon.org/@TauriApps&quot;&gt;Mastodon&lt;/a&gt;, and &lt;a href=&quot;https://discord.com/invite/tauri&quot;&gt;Discord&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h2 id=&quot;getting-involved&quot;&gt;Getting Involved&lt;/h2&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;While we’re working towards Tauri 2.0 stable there are a lot of ways for the community to get involved. The more involvement and feedback we have, the better Tauri 2.0 will be for everyone. Here are just a few ways to get involved:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Test prerelease versions of Tauri and give feedback via Discord and GitHub issues.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Contribute to documentation and translations on &lt;a href=&quot;https://v2.tauri.app&quot;&gt;https://v2.tauri.app&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Help in the Tauri community by supporting others in Discord and GitHub issues and giving feedback for major issues that arise.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Begin planning content around Tauri 2.0 (although we recommend waiting until at least 2.0 beta). Reach out to the Tauri Working Group on Discord if you would like to collaborate on content ideas such as videos, blogs, courses, or anything else.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;hr&gt;
&lt;p&gt;We hope this provides a bit of transparency about where we’re at, where we’re headed, and what you can do to help us get there. Give us your feedback by &lt;a href=&quot;https://discord.com/invite/tauri&quot;&gt;joining us on Discord&lt;/a&gt; and &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri-docs/discussions/1522&quot;&gt;joining the GitHub Discussion for this post&lt;/a&gt;. Happy building!&lt;/p&gt;
</content:encoded></item><item><title>Tauri Board Elections &amp; Governance Update</title><link>https://v2.tauri.app/blog/tauri-board-elections-and-governance-updates/</link><guid isPermaLink="true">https://v2.tauri.app/blog/tauri-board-elections-and-governance-updates/</guid><description>The Tauri Programme is celebrating it&apos;s two year anniversary of Tauri becoming a programme within The Commons Conservancy as well as the one year milestone of the Tauri 1.0 release. This also means we&apos;re preparing for the next round of Tauri Board Director elections to welcome in the next chapter of Tauri.</description><pubDate>Thu, 15 Jun 2023 00:00:00 GMT</pubDate><content:encoded>&lt;p&gt;The Tauri Programme is celebrating it’s two year anniversary of Tauri becoming a programme within &lt;a href=&quot;https://commonsconservancy.org/&quot;&gt;The Commons Conservancy&lt;/a&gt; as well as the one year milestone of the &lt;a href=&quot;https://v2.tauri.app/blog/tauri-1-0/&quot;&gt;Tauri 1.0 release&lt;/a&gt;. This also means we’re preparing for the next round of Tauri Board Director elections to welcome in the next chapter of Tauri.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;We wanted to celebrate these milestones but also use this as a chance to reflect on how we’ve grown since the Tauri 1.0 launch and how to continue evolving Tauri. The Tauri Working Group has been building the next iteration of the governance model for the past several months to make it more accessible for new and existing contributors, and ultimately sustainable for the future of Tauri.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h2 id=&quot;board-elections&quot;&gt;Board Elections&lt;/h2&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The Tauri Board of Directors is the central decision-making body for the Tauri Programme and is responsible for the overall health and stability of the Tauri Programme. Additional details can be found on the &lt;a href=&quot;https://tauri.app/about/governance&quot;&gt;Tauri Governance page&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;In order to provide continuity from one year to another, the elections of seats are staggered over the course of 2 years. This means that one year a portion of the seats are elected, and the following year the remaining seats are elected. A Tauri Director seat is an elected position with a term of 2 years. The Board has a minimum of 3 Directors and a maximum of 7. This year there are 5 seats open for election.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h3 id=&quot;diversity&quot;&gt;Diversity&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;This year we’d like to focus on diversifying the Tauri Board. We’re looking for candidates that may be a developer who’s worked on Tauri, a stakeholder in Tauri’s future, experienced individuals from industry, or a person with a passion for regulatory and legal aspects within open source. &lt;strong&gt;If any or all of these are of interest to you then we’d invite you to apply as a candidate.&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h3 id=&quot;applying-for-candidacy&quot;&gt;Applying for Candidacy&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;There are 3 steps to express interest and apply for candidacy:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ol&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Learn about the role of a Tauri Board Director on the &lt;a href=&quot;https://tauri.app/about/governance#tauri-board--board-directors&quot;&gt;Governance page&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Prepare a written introduction about yourself that covers who you are, your history and relevance for Tauri, and what you would like to bring to the Board.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Apply prior to July 2nd, 2023&lt;/strong&gt; by emailing &lt;a href=&quot;mailto:board@tauri.app&quot;&gt;board@tauri.app&lt;/a&gt;, messaging the &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;@board&lt;/code&gt; role on the &lt;a href=&quot;https://discord.com/invite/tauri&quot;&gt;Tauri Discord&lt;/a&gt;, or by directly messaging Daniel Thompson-Yvetot on Discord (&lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;@Denjell&lt;/code&gt;) or email (&lt;a href=&quot;mailto:denjell@tauri.app&quot;&gt;denjell@tauri.app&lt;/a&gt;).&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ol&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Voting will take place starting on July 5th through July 12th, 2023 where Tauri Working Group Members will cast their votes. We’re expecting to announce the results on or before July 16, 2023. If elected, we’ll ask you to sign &lt;a href=&quot;https://dracc.commonsconservancy.org/0016/&quot;&gt;this pledge&lt;/a&gt; if you haven’t done so previously.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;If you have any questions or would like more information please reach out by emailing &lt;a href=&quot;mailto:board@tauri.app&quot;&gt;board@tauri.app&lt;/a&gt;, messaging the &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;@board&lt;/code&gt; role on the &lt;a href=&quot;https://discord.com/invite/tauri&quot;&gt;Tauri Discord&lt;/a&gt;, or by directly messaging Daniel Thompson-Yvetot on Discord (&lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;@Denjell&lt;/code&gt;) or email (&lt;a href=&quot;mailto:denjell@tauri.app&quot;&gt;denjell@tauri.app&lt;/a&gt;).&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;hr&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h2 id=&quot;governance-update&quot;&gt;Governance Update&lt;/h2&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Tauri has always had a Working Group that’s responsible for the day-to-day direction and execution, but as we grew it became clear that we needed to evolve the Working Group. We also wanted to give new Working Group members a clear places to jump in and get started. We thought of how we could achieve this while building and iterating on the existing framework. &lt;strong&gt;With these goals in mind, we created two new concepts within the Working Group: Domains and Domain Leads.&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h3 id=&quot;domains&quot;&gt;Domains&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;A Domain in the Working Group represents a specific area of interest. We’re starting with these 4 domains:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
&lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Development&lt;/strong&gt;: Developing and maintaining the Tauri software&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Community&lt;/strong&gt;: Looks after the broader community resources and the public presence of Tauri&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Governance &amp;#x26; Guidance&lt;/strong&gt;: Involved with all things organizational in nature&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Operations&lt;/strong&gt;: Responsible for all the tooling and infrastructure Tauri needs to get work done&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;p&gt;This gives clear entry points for a new member of the Working Group to jump into right away. It also allows us to continue growing our core competency of core Tauri development while giving additional focus to all areas which support the Tauri organization as a whole. Each of these domains will be represented by Domain Members and Domain Leads, but membership is not mutually exclusive.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h3 id=&quot;domain-leads&quot;&gt;Domain Leads&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Within a domain, there is a position for 2-3 Domain Leads. These are core individuals in the Working Group that are critical to their respective Domain. They help by setting the direction and priorities for their domain while also serving as key leaders for the Working Group as a whole.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Leads are appointed twice a year, in the spring and in the fall. Since this governance update is being rolled out mid-term we’re appointing interim leads that have been key members of the Working Group. This fall we’ll reflect on the initial experience and host a new round to appoint the Domain Leads. We’ll announce information for that process when we’re closer to “appointment season”.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;hr&gt;
&lt;p&gt;We hope that these changes provide the foundation to continue the journey the Tauri Working Group has been on while also catalyzing the continued growth and sustainability of the organization. You can learn more about these updates in the &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/governance-and-guidance&quot;&gt;Governance and Guidance repo on GitHub&lt;/a&gt;. Give us your feedback on the &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri-docs/discussions/1297&quot;&gt;GitHub Discussion for this post&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;
</content:encoded></item><item><title>Announcing Tauri 1.4.0</title><link>https://v2.tauri.app/blog/tauri-1-4/</link><guid isPermaLink="true">https://v2.tauri.app/blog/tauri-1-4/</guid><description>The Tauri team is happy to announce the 1.4.0 release.</description><pubDate>Wed, 14 Jun 2023 00:00:00 GMT</pubDate><content:encoded>&lt;p&gt;&lt;img src=&quot;https://v2.tauri.app/_astro/header.pm2INrN3_Z1KxkA5.webp&quot; alt=&quot;Tauri 1.4 Launch Hero Image&quot; loading=&quot;lazy&quot; decoding=&quot;async&quot; width=&quot;1280&quot; height=&quot;640&quot;&gt;&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The Tauri team is excited to announce the 1.4 release. This version includes several new features and important bug fixes such as CLI completions, unit testing capabilities and Windows installer improvements.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h2 id=&quot;upgrading&quot;&gt;Upgrading&lt;/h2&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Make sure to update both NPM and Cargo dependencies to the 1.4.0 release. You can update the dependencies with:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;starlight-tabs data-sync-key=&quot;cmd&quot;&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;ul role=&quot;tablist&quot;&gt;&lt;li role=&quot;presentation&quot;&gt;&lt;a role=&quot;tab&quot; href=&quot;#tab-panel-4205&quot; id=&quot;tab-4205&quot; aria-selected=&quot;true&quot; tabindex=&quot;0&quot;&gt;npm&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li role=&quot;presentation&quot;&gt;&lt;a role=&quot;tab&quot; href=&quot;#tab-panel-4206&quot; id=&quot;tab-4206&quot; aria-selected=&quot;false&quot; tabindex=&quot;-1&quot;&gt;yarn&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li role=&quot;presentation&quot;&gt;&lt;a role=&quot;tab&quot; href=&quot;#tab-panel-4207&quot; id=&quot;tab-4207&quot; aria-selected=&quot;false&quot; tabindex=&quot;-1&quot;&gt;pnpm&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li role=&quot;presentation&quot;&gt;&lt;a role=&quot;tab&quot; href=&quot;#tab-panel-4208&quot; id=&quot;tab-4208&quot; aria-selected=&quot;false&quot; tabindex=&quot;-1&quot;&gt;cargo&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div id=&quot;tab-panel-4205&quot; aria-labelledby=&quot;tab-4205&quot; role=&quot;tabpanel&quot;&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;figure&gt;&lt;figcaption&gt;&lt;/figcaption&gt;&lt;pre&gt;&lt;code&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;npm&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;install&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;@tauri-apps/cli@latest&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;@tauri-apps/api@latest&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/figure&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div id=&quot;tab-panel-4206&quot; aria-labelledby=&quot;tab-4206&quot; role=&quot;tabpanel&quot; hidden&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;figure&gt;&lt;figcaption&gt;&lt;/figcaption&gt;&lt;pre&gt;&lt;code&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;yarn&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;upgrade&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;@tauri-apps/cli&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;@tauri-apps/api&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;--latest&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/figure&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div id=&quot;tab-panel-4207&quot; aria-labelledby=&quot;tab-4207&quot; role=&quot;tabpanel&quot; hidden&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;figure&gt;&lt;figcaption&gt;&lt;/figcaption&gt;&lt;pre&gt;&lt;code&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;pnpm&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;update&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;@tauri-apps/cli&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;@tauri-apps/api&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;--latest&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/figure&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div id=&quot;tab-panel-4208&quot; aria-labelledby=&quot;tab-4208&quot; role=&quot;tabpanel&quot; hidden&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;figure&gt;&lt;figcaption&gt;&lt;/figcaption&gt;&lt;pre&gt;&lt;code&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;cargo&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;update&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/figure&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;starlight-tabs-restore&gt;&lt;/starlight-tabs-restore&gt;&lt;/starlight-tabs&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h2 id=&quot;whats-in-140&quot;&gt;What’s in 1.4.0&lt;/h2&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h3 id=&quot;cli-completions&quot;&gt;CLI completions&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The Tauri CLI now can generate shell completions for Bash, Zsh, PowerShell and Fish. See the &lt;a href=&quot;https://tauri.app/v1/api/cli#completions&quot;&gt;documentation&lt;/a&gt; for more information.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h3 id=&quot;disabling-window-controls&quot;&gt;Disabling window controls&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The window’s maximize, minimize and close buttons now can be disabled via configuration or API calls. Check out &lt;a href=&quot;https://docs.rs/tauri/1/tauri/window/struct.Window.html#method.set_maximizable&quot;&gt;&lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;set_maximizable&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href=&quot;https://docs.rs/tauri/1/tauri/window/struct.Window.html#method.set_minimizable&quot;&gt;&lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;set_minimizable&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/a&gt; and &lt;a href=&quot;https://docs.rs/tauri/1/tauri/window/struct.Window.html#method.set_closable&quot;&gt;&lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;set_closable&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/a&gt; for the Rust APIs and &lt;a href=&quot;https://tauri.app/v1/api/js/window#setmaximizable&quot;&gt;&lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;setMaximizable&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href=&quot;https://tauri.app/v1/api/js/window#setminimizable&quot;&gt;&lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;setMinimizable&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/a&gt; and &lt;a href=&quot;https://tauri.app/v1/api/js/window#setclosable&quot;&gt;&lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;setClosable&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/a&gt; for the JavaScript APIs.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h3 id=&quot;nsis-improvements&quot;&gt;NSIS improvements&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The 1.4.0 release includes several NSIS bundle enhancements:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Custom language files&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Custom installer template (.nsi file)&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Support for the dutch, japanese, korean, persian, swedish and turkish languages&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;If your application is installed via WiX, the installer will prompt the user to uninstall it&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Improved support to updater install modes&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;p&gt;See the &lt;a href=&quot;https://tauri.app/v1/guides/developing/windows#customizing-the-nsis-installer-template&quot;&gt;installer customization guide&lt;/a&gt; and &lt;a href=&quot;https://tauri.app/v1/guides/developing/windows#internationalization&quot;&gt;installer internationalization&lt;/a&gt; for more information.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h3 id=&quot;msrv-change&quot;&gt;MSRV change&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Tauri 1.4 still has a minimum supported Rust version of 1.60, but due to some dependency incompatibility issues we are no longer pinning the patch version of the &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;time&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;ignore&lt;/code&gt;, and &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;winnow&lt;/code&gt; crates. If you are still using Rust 1.60, you will need to pin these versions manually with &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/blob/076e1a81a50468e3dfb34ae9ca7e77c5e1758daa/.github/workflows/test-core.yml#L88&quot;&gt;cargo update&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h3 id=&quot;unit-tests&quot;&gt;Unit tests&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri&lt;/code&gt; crate now exposes the &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;test&lt;/code&gt; module under the &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;test&lt;/code&gt; Cargo feature. This module is still unstable but allows you to unit test your application by creating a &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri::App&lt;/code&gt; instance that can execute without spawning windows. See the &lt;a href=&quot;https://docs.rs/tauri/1/tauri/develop/tests/index.html&quot;&gt;documentation&lt;/a&gt; for more information and examples.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h3 id=&quot;other-changes&quot;&gt;Other changes&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Starting on v1.4.0, our changelog format has been improved. Check out the entire list of changes:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
&lt;li&gt;&lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/releases/tag/tauri-v1.4.0&quot;&gt;tauri&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;&lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/releases/tag/tauri-cli-v1.4.0&quot;&gt;tauri-cli&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;&lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/releases/tag/tauri-bundler-v1.3.0&quot;&gt;tauri-bundler&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h2 id=&quot;audit&quot;&gt;Audit&lt;/h2&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The &lt;em&gt;internal&lt;/em&gt;&lt;sup&gt;&lt;a href=&quot;#user-content-fn-1&quot; id=&quot;user-content-fnref-1&quot; data-footnote-ref=&quot;&quot; aria-describedby=&quot;footnote-label&quot;&gt;1&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/sup&gt; audit was performed by Tillmann &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tillmann-crabnebula&quot;&gt;@tillmann-crabnebula&lt;/a&gt;
and Chip &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/chip-crabnebula&quot;&gt;@chip-crabnebula&lt;/a&gt;,
who are also involved in security topics at the project under their private handles (&lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tweidinger&quot;&gt;@tweidinger&lt;/a&gt; and &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/chippers&quot;&gt;@chippers&lt;/a&gt;).&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;It was performed during paid time at &lt;a href=&quot;https://crabnebula.dev&quot;&gt;CrabNebula Ltd.&lt;/a&gt; and we are grateful to be able
to spend parts of our work time contributing to the open source project and making it a more secure environment :heart:.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;For this release we manually audited a selection of &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/issues?q=label%3A%22security%3A%20reviewed%22%20is%3Aclosed&quot;&gt;PRs&lt;/a&gt;
instead of all PRs coming into the release.
The new approach means the reviewers and developers need to decide on their own if a PR is introducing any security relevant change.
A review can be triggered by anyone involved in the change, by adding a label to the PR.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;For the first time we also audited after the official release due to time constraints.
This resulted in a &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/security/advisories/GHSA-wmff-grcw-jcfm&quot;&gt;security patch&lt;/a&gt; release, fixing the only impactful issue (CVE-2023-34460) discovered during auditing.
In general this release was more focused on fixing and improving the NSIS features and introduced less new features and security relevant changes.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;section data-footnotes=&quot;&quot;&gt;
&lt;ol&gt;
&lt;li id=&quot;user-content-fn-1&quot;&gt;
&lt;p&gt;It is internal in the sense that we are also involved in the project itself but performed with the help of an external entity. Calling it external security audit would create false impressions. &lt;a href=&quot;#user-content-fnref-1&quot; data-footnote-backref=&quot;&quot; aria-label=&quot;Back to reference 1&quot;&gt;↩&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ol&gt;
&lt;/section&gt;</content:encoded></item><item><title>Announcing Tauri 1.3.0</title><link>https://v2.tauri.app/blog/tauri-1-3/</link><guid isPermaLink="true">https://v2.tauri.app/blog/tauri-1-3/</guid><description>The Tauri team is happy to announce the 1.3.0 release.</description><pubDate>Wed, 03 May 2023 00:00:00 GMT</pubDate><content:encoded>&lt;p&gt;&lt;img src=&quot;https://v2.tauri.app/_astro/header.w5CMWRUj_ZAr4g1.webp&quot; alt=&quot;Tauri 1.3 Launch Hero Image&quot; loading=&quot;lazy&quot; decoding=&quot;async&quot; width=&quot;1280&quot; height=&quot;640&quot;&gt;&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The Tauri team is excited to announce the 1.3 release. This version includes security improvements, new features and important bug fixes.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h2 id=&quot;upgrading&quot;&gt;Upgrading&lt;/h2&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Make sure to update both NPM and Cargo dependencies to the 1.3.0 release. You can update the dependencies with:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;starlight-tabs data-sync-key=&quot;cmd&quot;&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;ul role=&quot;tablist&quot;&gt;&lt;li role=&quot;presentation&quot;&gt;&lt;a role=&quot;tab&quot; href=&quot;#tab-panel-4209&quot; id=&quot;tab-4209&quot; aria-selected=&quot;true&quot; tabindex=&quot;0&quot;&gt;npm&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li role=&quot;presentation&quot;&gt;&lt;a role=&quot;tab&quot; href=&quot;#tab-panel-4210&quot; id=&quot;tab-4210&quot; aria-selected=&quot;false&quot; tabindex=&quot;-1&quot;&gt;yarn&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li role=&quot;presentation&quot;&gt;&lt;a role=&quot;tab&quot; href=&quot;#tab-panel-4211&quot; id=&quot;tab-4211&quot; aria-selected=&quot;false&quot; tabindex=&quot;-1&quot;&gt;pnpm&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li role=&quot;presentation&quot;&gt;&lt;a role=&quot;tab&quot; href=&quot;#tab-panel-4212&quot; id=&quot;tab-4212&quot; aria-selected=&quot;false&quot; tabindex=&quot;-1&quot;&gt;cargo&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div id=&quot;tab-panel-4209&quot; aria-labelledby=&quot;tab-4209&quot; role=&quot;tabpanel&quot;&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;figure&gt;&lt;figcaption&gt;&lt;/figcaption&gt;&lt;pre&gt;&lt;code&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;npm&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;install&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;@tauri-apps/cli@latest&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;@tauri-apps/api@latest&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/figure&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div id=&quot;tab-panel-4210&quot; aria-labelledby=&quot;tab-4210&quot; role=&quot;tabpanel&quot; hidden&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;figure&gt;&lt;figcaption&gt;&lt;/figcaption&gt;&lt;pre&gt;&lt;code&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;yarn&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;upgrade&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;@tauri-apps/cli&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;@tauri-apps/api&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;--latest&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/figure&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div id=&quot;tab-panel-4211&quot; aria-labelledby=&quot;tab-4211&quot; role=&quot;tabpanel&quot; hidden&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;figure&gt;&lt;figcaption&gt;&lt;/figcaption&gt;&lt;pre&gt;&lt;code&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;pnpm&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;update&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;@tauri-apps/cli&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;@tauri-apps/api&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;--latest&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/figure&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div id=&quot;tab-panel-4212&quot; aria-labelledby=&quot;tab-4212&quot; role=&quot;tabpanel&quot; hidden&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;figure&gt;&lt;figcaption&gt;&lt;/figcaption&gt;&lt;pre&gt;&lt;code&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;cargo&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;update&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/figure&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;starlight-tabs-restore&gt;&lt;/starlight-tabs-restore&gt;&lt;/starlight-tabs&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h2 id=&quot;whats-in-130&quot;&gt;What’s in 1.3.0&lt;/h2&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h3 id=&quot;nsis&quot;&gt;NSIS&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The Tauri CLI can now create Windows application installers using NSIS. This new bundle target is also available on macOS and Linux as an experimental feature, so you can cross-compile your Windows installer. Documentation on the latter will be published soon.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h3 id=&quot;tauri-13-audit&quot;&gt;Tauri 1.3 Audit&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The internal audit was performed by &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tweidinger&quot;&gt;@tweidinger&lt;/a&gt; and &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/chippers&quot;&gt;@chippers&lt;/a&gt;, who are involved in most security topics at the Tauri project. It was performed during paid time at &lt;a href=&quot;https://crabnebula.dev/?utm_campaign=tauri-1-3&amp;#x26;utm_source=tauri&quot;&gt;CrabNebula&lt;/a&gt; and we are grateful to be able to spend parts of our work time contributing to the open source project and making it a more secure environment :heart:.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;We manually audited over 45 &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/pull/5815&quot;&gt;PRs&lt;/a&gt;. Some PRs (e.g. &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/pull/5544&quot;&gt;#5544&lt;/a&gt;) lead us to diving into very old RFCs (&lt;a href=&quot;https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6068#page-12&quot;&gt;RFC6068&lt;/a&gt; and &lt;a href=&quot;https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3966#section-11&quot;&gt;RFC3966&lt;/a&gt;), &lt;a href=&quot;https://nsis.sourceforge.io/Docs/&quot;&gt;NSIS documentation&lt;/a&gt; (e.g. &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/pull/6039&quot;&gt;#6039&lt;/a&gt;) and many other external resources. We documented questions, notes and findings in markdown files and shared these notes with the responsible developers to ensure appropriate fixes.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The changes from a security perspective and findings of the audit are summarized in the following sub-sections.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h3 id=&quot;external-api-access-5918&quot;&gt;External API Access &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/pull/5918&quot;&gt;#5918&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;This was by far the most impactful and time consuming PR we investigated. This PR introduces a streamlined way for applications to allow external domains access to the Tauri IPC layer&lt;sup&gt;&lt;a href=&quot;#user-content-fn-1&quot; id=&quot;user-content-fnref-1&quot; data-footnote-ref=&quot;&quot; aria-describedby=&quot;footnote-label&quot;&gt;1&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/sup&gt; and usage majorly impacts the security model of a Tauri application. Security impact&lt;sup&gt;&lt;a href=&quot;#user-content-fn-2&quot; id=&quot;user-content-fnref-2&quot; data-footnote-ref=&quot;&quot; aria-describedby=&quot;footnote-label&quot;&gt;2&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/sup&gt; depends on exposure&lt;sup&gt;&lt;a href=&quot;#user-content-fn-3&quot; id=&quot;user-content-fnref-3&quot; data-footnote-ref=&quot;&quot; aria-describedby=&quot;footnote-label&quot;&gt;3&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/sup&gt; of the feature, enabled Tauri commands and the capabilities of an adversary&lt;sup&gt;&lt;a href=&quot;#user-content-fn-4&quot; id=&quot;user-content-fnref-4&quot; data-footnote-ref=&quot;&quot; aria-describedby=&quot;footnote-label&quot;&gt;4&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/sup&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;We consider this new feature similar to driving a very fast race car without any safety features enabled and urge developers to &lt;em&gt;very very carefully&lt;/em&gt; consider if they really need this exposure.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Before this addition was merged, a semi-known vulnerability was (ab)used by application developers to achieve the same functionality. To make the whole community aware of this risk we published a &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/security/advisories/GHSA-4wm2-cwcf-wwvp&quot;&gt;security advisory&lt;/a&gt; to give a heads up. Applications are affected if they allow users to navigate to arbitrary domains or have an open redirect vulnerability&lt;sup&gt;&lt;a href=&quot;#user-content-fn-5&quot; id=&quot;user-content-fnref-5&quot; data-footnote-ref=&quot;&quot; aria-describedby=&quot;footnote-label&quot;&gt;5&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/sup&gt;. If you implement such a feature you should update to the 1.3 release as fast as possible.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The initial PR changes allowed wildcards (&lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;https://*&lt;/code&gt;) and glob patterns, which we believe are helpful but shouldn’t be exposed to all Tauri developers. We concluded that the risk of over-exposure, like the allow list toggle to enable all Tauri API endpoints, does not justify this permissive exposure. The final implementation allows to configure specified &lt;em&gt;(sub)domains&lt;/em&gt;&lt;sup&gt;&lt;a href=&quot;#user-content-fn-6&quot; id=&quot;user-content-fnref-6&quot; data-footnote-ref=&quot;&quot; aria-describedby=&quot;footnote-label&quot;&gt;6&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/sup&gt; (eg: &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;example.com&lt;/code&gt;) to gain remote access to the Tauri IPC.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The few edge use cases, which require wildcards or even further exposure can be implemented by custom Rust code which is able to dynamically modify the IPC access. We now expose this remote IPC scope in a similar fashion as the &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;fs&lt;/code&gt; or &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;http&lt;/code&gt; scope.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Assuming a &lt;strong&gt;fully trusted&lt;/strong&gt; web service on &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;https://trusted.example&lt;/code&gt; it is now possible to configure the security scope to allow certain windows or even plugins access to custom implemented commands and optionally the inbuilt Tauri API:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;figure&gt;&lt;figcaption&gt;&lt;/figcaption&gt;&lt;pre&gt;&lt;code&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;&quot;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;security&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;&quot;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;: {&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;  &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;&quot;dangerousRemoteUrlIpcAccess&quot;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;: [&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;&lt;span&gt;    &lt;/span&gt;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;{&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;      &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;&quot;windows&quot;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;: [&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;&quot;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;main&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;&quot;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;, &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;&quot;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;settings&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;&quot;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;],&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;      &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;&quot;domain&quot;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;: &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;&quot;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;trusted.example&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;&quot;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;,&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;      &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;&quot;plugins&quot;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;: [&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;&quot;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;trusted-plugin&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;&quot;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;],&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;      &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;&quot;enableTauriAPI&quot;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;: &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;false&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;&lt;span&gt;    &lt;/span&gt;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;},&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;&lt;span&gt;  &lt;/span&gt;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;],&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;}&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/figure&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Shared domains &lt;strong&gt;MUST NOT&lt;/strong&gt; be used for this in any circumstances. We do not limit access to paths or specific files. You can only scope with &lt;strong&gt;trusted&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;em&gt;(sub)domains&lt;/em&gt;&lt;sup&gt;&lt;a href=&quot;#user-content-fn-6&quot; id=&quot;user-content-fnref-6-2&quot; data-footnote-ref=&quot;&quot; aria-describedby=&quot;footnote-label&quot;&gt;6&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/sup&gt;. Another very risky catch is that developers must be sure that the domain ownership does not change over the lifetime of the application. Domain takeover could lead to compromised user devices.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h3 id=&quot;browser-arguments-5799&quot;&gt;Browser Arguments &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/pull/5799&quot;&gt;#5799&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Due to certain webview features not enabled nor being accessible, a community contribution introduced the possibility to add additional arguments to the webview process, which is created in a new window.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;This feature was exposed to the frontend in the &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;window&lt;/code&gt; endpoint. We found that this exposure was highly risky, as most webviews have very impactful features and flags that can be allowed via the process arguments.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;All of the following threat model assumptions are based on the Tauri window creation being allowed in the &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;allowlist&lt;/code&gt; of the &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri.conf&lt;/code&gt; and therefore exposed to the frontend. This PR affects Windows only, therefore there is no impact on the other supported operating systems.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;An adversary with the capabilities to create windows and pass command line arguments to the webview can elevate their privileges to escape the strict sandboxing of Tauri and the webview.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The flags allow to enable several dangerous webview features, from loading profiles outside of the current default profile folder (stealing browser sessions from the device) to disabling security measurements of the webview (eg: certificate validation, sandboxing, webdriver/headless mode, device management endpoints, …).&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;We found an old but gold and still unique documentation reference at &lt;a href=&quot;https://peter.sh/experiments/chromium-command-line-switches/&quot;&gt;https://peter.sh/experiments/chromium-command-line-switches/&lt;/a&gt;, which helped us understand possible risks on Windows, as the Webview2 uses the same flags.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The feature was then changed to be only exposed on the rust side. Tauri application developers can use this to implement custom commands to invoke webview windows with use case specific arguments.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h3 id=&quot;possible-zipslip-4674&quot;&gt;Possible ZipSlip &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/pull/4674&quot;&gt;#4674&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;We found that the components to extract remote bundler files like the Webview2 installer were manually extracting single files with the &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/blob/d1179c78e2ce04067461461868f66d2020802f4b/tooling/bundler/src/bundle/windows/util.rs#L116&quot;&gt;&lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;extract_zip&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/a&gt; function, which uses &lt;a href=&quot;https://docs.rs/zip/0.6/zip/read/struct.ZipFile.html#method.name&quot;&gt;&lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;ZipFile::name()&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/a&gt; instead of &lt;a href=&quot;https://docs.rs/zip/0.6/zip/read/struct.ZipFile.html#method.enclosed_name&quot;&gt;&lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;ZipFile::enclosed_name()&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/a&gt; as recommended in the documentation. Files which had names like &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;../../../../foo.sh&lt;/code&gt; could be extracted outside of the intended directory on the filesystem. This kind of vulnerability is called &lt;a href=&quot;https://security.snyk.io/research/zip-slip-vulnerability&quot;&gt;ZipSlip&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;As the function was only used on verified and trusted files the impact here was nearly zero. Regardless we changed the implementation to facilitate the proper extraction method.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h3 id=&quot;bundler-hardening-6039&quot;&gt;Bundler Hardening &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/pull/6039&quot;&gt;#6039&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The bundler was not escaping content passed to the &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/blob/35b587c830140609004732d713b8055f38d643d8/tooling/bundler/src/bundle/windows/nsis.rs#L346&quot;&gt;&lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;handlebars::Handlebars::render()&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, which could cause unwanted code execution during the bundler phase. This was also a low impact issue but was promptly fixed.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h3 id=&quot;other-changes&quot;&gt;Other changes&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h4 id=&quot;new&quot;&gt;New&lt;/h4&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
&lt;li&gt;&lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;additional_browser_args&lt;/code&gt; option when creating windows &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/pull/5799&quot;&gt;#5799&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Add &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;is_minimized()&lt;/code&gt; window method. &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/pull/5618&quot;&gt;#5618&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Add &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;title&lt;/code&gt; getter on window. &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/pull/5515&quot;&gt;#5515&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;content protection APIs &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/pull/5513&quot;&gt;#5513&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;Builder::device_event_filter&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;App::set_device_event_filter&lt;/code&gt; methods. &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/pull/5562&quot;&gt;#5562&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Add &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;WindowsAttributes::app_manifest&lt;/code&gt; to specify the application manifest on Windows. &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/pull/5730&quot;&gt;#5730&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Add support for Cargo’s workspace inheritance. &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/pull/5775&quot;&gt;#5775&lt;/a&gt; &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/pull/6144&quot;&gt;#6144&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added window’s &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;url()&lt;/code&gt; getter. &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/pull/5914&quot;&gt;#5914&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;Window::on_navigation&lt;/code&gt;. &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/pull/5686&quot;&gt;#5686&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Allow setting the text of the dialog buttons. &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/pull/4383&quot;&gt;#4383&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Implement &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;SystemTray::with_tooltip&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;SystemTrayHandle::set_tooltip&lt;/code&gt; for Windows and macOS. &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/pull/5938&quot;&gt;#5938&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Add dylib support to &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri.bundle.macOS.frameworks&lt;/code&gt;. &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/pull/5732&quot;&gt;#5732&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h4 id=&quot;enhancements&quot;&gt;Enhancements&lt;/h4&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
&lt;li&gt;On Windows, the &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;msi&lt;/code&gt; installer’s &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;Launch App&lt;/code&gt; checkbox will be checked by default. &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/pull/5871&quot;&gt;#5871&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Add &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;--png&lt;/code&gt; option for the &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;icon&lt;/code&gt; command to generate custom icon sizes. &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/pull/5246&quot;&gt;#5246&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;On Windows, change webview theme based on Window theme for more accurate &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;prefers-color-scheme&lt;/code&gt; support. &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/pull/5874&quot;&gt;#5874&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Remove default features from Cargo.toml template. &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/pull/6074&quot;&gt;#6074&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Add a method to the &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;WindowBuilder&lt;/code&gt; struct to recreate windows from tauri.conf.json configurations.&lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/pull/6073&quot;&gt;#6073&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Improve the error message when &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;rustc&lt;/code&gt; couldn’t be found. &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/pull/6021&quot;&gt;#6021&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Added support for pre-release identifiers and build numbers for the &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;.msi&lt;/code&gt; bundle target. Only one of each can be used and it must be numeric only. The version must still be semver compatible according to &lt;a href=&quot;https://semver.org/&quot;&gt;https://semver.org/&lt;/a&gt;. &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/pull/6096&quot;&gt;#6096&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Add &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;--ci&lt;/code&gt; flag and respect the &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;CI&lt;/code&gt; environment variable on the &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;signer generate&lt;/code&gt; command. In this case the default password will be an empty string and the CLI will not prompt for a value. &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/pull/6097&quot;&gt;#6097&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Skip the password prompt on the build command when &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;TAURI_KEY_PASSWORD&lt;/code&gt; environment variable is empty and the &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;--ci&lt;/code&gt; argument is provided or the &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;CI&lt;/code&gt; environment variable is set. &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/commit/d4f89af18d69fd95a4d8a1ede8442547c6a6d0ee&quot;&gt;d4f89af18d69fd95a4d8a1ede8442547c6a6d0ee&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h4 id=&quot;fixes&quot;&gt;Fixes&lt;/h4&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Fix &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri info&lt;/code&gt; panicking when parsing crates version on a newly created project without a &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;Cargo.lock&lt;/code&gt; file. &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/pull/5873&quot;&gt;#5873&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Fix building apps with unicode characters in their &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;productName&lt;/code&gt;. &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/pull/5872&quot;&gt;#5872&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Sync &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;__TAURI_METADATA__.__windows&lt;/code&gt; across all windows. &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/pull/5615&quot;&gt;#5615&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Fix resize glitch when double clicking a custom titlebar in the top resize area. &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/pull/5966&quot;&gt;#5966&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Disable cursor mouse events on Linux. &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/pull/6025&quot;&gt;#6025&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;Fix serialization of js &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;Map&lt;/code&gt; when used in &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;invoke&lt;/code&gt;. &lt;a href=&quot;https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/pull/6099&quot;&gt;#6099&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;section data-footnotes=&quot;&quot;&gt;
&lt;ol&gt;
&lt;li id=&quot;user-content-fn-1&quot;&gt;
&lt;p&gt;&lt;em&gt;Inter-Process Communication&lt;/em&gt;, in this instance the communication between the Tauri core and the frontend code run inside the webview. &lt;a href=&quot;#user-content-fnref-1&quot; data-footnote-backref=&quot;&quot; aria-label=&quot;Back to reference 1&quot;&gt;↩&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li id=&quot;user-content-fn-2&quot;&gt;
&lt;p&gt;&lt;em&gt;Security Impact&lt;/em&gt;: What is the theoretical biggest impact of this threat combination? This highly depends on correct scoping of Tauri API endpoints and hardening of custom implemented Tauri commands. &lt;a href=&quot;#user-content-fnref-2&quot; data-footnote-backref=&quot;&quot; aria-label=&quot;Back to reference 2&quot;&gt;↩&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li id=&quot;user-content-fn-3&quot;&gt;
&lt;p&gt;&lt;em&gt;Exposure&lt;/em&gt;: Describes the exposed scope items of this feature to either an user or adversary. It is possible to restrict exposure to only certain domains, windows or only to custom implemented commands. &lt;a href=&quot;#user-content-fnref-3&quot; data-footnote-backref=&quot;&quot; aria-label=&quot;Back to reference 3&quot;&gt;↩&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li id=&quot;user-content-fn-4&quot;&gt;
&lt;p&gt;&lt;em&gt;Adversary Capabilities&lt;/em&gt;: Which kind of privileges has the adversary? Can range from tricking user into entering malicious input to code execution in the frontend via cross-site-scripting (which is the highest privilege for frontend code in our case). Common capabilities are described in the &lt;a href=&quot;https://owasp.org/www-community/attacks/&quot;&gt;OWASP documentation&lt;/a&gt;. &lt;a href=&quot;#user-content-fnref-4&quot; data-footnote-backref=&quot;&quot; aria-label=&quot;Back to reference 4&quot;&gt;↩&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li id=&quot;user-content-fn-5&quot;&gt;
&lt;p&gt;&lt;em&gt;An application can be exploited if it parses user input for making an URL redirection decision, which is then not properly validated.&lt;/em&gt; &lt;a href=&quot;https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Open_redirect&quot;&gt;Wikipedia Source&lt;/a&gt; &lt;a href=&quot;#user-content-fnref-5&quot; data-footnote-backref=&quot;&quot; aria-label=&quot;Back to reference 5&quot;&gt;↩&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li id=&quot;user-content-fn-6&quot;&gt;
&lt;p&gt;see the &lt;a href=&quot;https://docs.rs/reqwest/latest/reqwest/struct.Url.html#method.domain&quot;&gt;Reqwest reference&lt;/a&gt; &lt;a href=&quot;#user-content-fnref-6&quot; data-footnote-backref=&quot;&quot; aria-label=&quot;Back to reference 6&quot;&gt;↩&lt;/a&gt; &lt;a href=&quot;#user-content-fnref-6-2&quot; data-footnote-backref=&quot;&quot; aria-label=&quot;Back to reference 6-2&quot;&gt;↩&lt;sup&gt;2&lt;/sup&gt;&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ol&gt;
&lt;/section&gt;</content:encoded></item><item><title>Tauri 2.0.0-alpha.4 Released</title><link>https://v2.tauri.app/blog/tauri-2-0-0-alpha-4/</link><guid isPermaLink="true">https://v2.tauri.app/blog/tauri-2-0-0-alpha-4/</guid><description>Mobile APIs for Tauri plugins! The 2.0.0-alpha.4 release has been published.</description><pubDate>Mon, 20 Mar 2023 00:00:00 GMT</pubDate><content:encoded>&lt;p&gt;&lt;img src=&quot;https://v2.tauri.app/_astro/header.DJC8YrJ3_rv4kd.webp&quot; alt=&quot;Tauri 1.2 Launch Hero Image&quot; loading=&quot;lazy&quot; decoding=&quot;async&quot; width=&quot;1280&quot; height=&quot;640&quot;&gt;&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;A new alpha release for the 2.0 has been published. This release includes all changes from the upcoming Tauri 1.3 release, an important breaking change on the HTTP client and native mobile capabilities for Tauri plugins.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h2 id=&quot;updating-dependencies&quot;&gt;Updating dependencies&lt;/h2&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Make sure to update both NPM and Cargo dependencies to the latest alpha release. You can update the NPM dependencies with:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;starlight-tabs data-sync-key=&quot;cmd&quot;&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;ul role=&quot;tablist&quot;&gt;&lt;li role=&quot;presentation&quot;&gt;&lt;a role=&quot;tab&quot; href=&quot;#tab-panel-4213&quot; id=&quot;tab-4213&quot; aria-selected=&quot;true&quot; tabindex=&quot;0&quot;&gt;npm&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li role=&quot;presentation&quot;&gt;&lt;a role=&quot;tab&quot; href=&quot;#tab-panel-4214&quot; id=&quot;tab-4214&quot; aria-selected=&quot;false&quot; tabindex=&quot;-1&quot;&gt;yarn&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li role=&quot;presentation&quot;&gt;&lt;a role=&quot;tab&quot; href=&quot;#tab-panel-4215&quot; id=&quot;tab-4215&quot; aria-selected=&quot;false&quot; tabindex=&quot;-1&quot;&gt;pnpm&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li role=&quot;presentation&quot;&gt;&lt;a role=&quot;tab&quot; href=&quot;#tab-panel-4216&quot; id=&quot;tab-4216&quot; aria-selected=&quot;false&quot; tabindex=&quot;-1&quot;&gt;cargo&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div id=&quot;tab-panel-4213&quot; aria-labelledby=&quot;tab-4213&quot; role=&quot;tabpanel&quot;&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;figure&gt;&lt;figcaption&gt;&lt;/figcaption&gt;&lt;pre&gt;&lt;code&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;npm&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;install&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;@tauri-apps/cli@next&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;@tauri-apps/api@next&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/figure&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div id=&quot;tab-panel-4214&quot; aria-labelledby=&quot;tab-4214&quot; role=&quot;tabpanel&quot; hidden&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;figure&gt;&lt;figcaption&gt;&lt;/figcaption&gt;&lt;pre&gt;&lt;code&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;yarn&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;upgrade&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;@tauri-apps/cli@next&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;@tauri-apps/api@next&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/figure&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div id=&quot;tab-panel-4215&quot; aria-labelledby=&quot;tab-4215&quot; role=&quot;tabpanel&quot; hidden&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;figure&gt;&lt;figcaption&gt;&lt;/figcaption&gt;&lt;pre&gt;&lt;code&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;pnpm&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;update&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;@tauri-apps/cli@next&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;@tauri-apps/api@next&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/figure&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div id=&quot;tab-panel-4216&quot; aria-labelledby=&quot;tab-4216&quot; role=&quot;tabpanel&quot; hidden&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;figure&gt;&lt;figcaption&gt;&lt;/figcaption&gt;&lt;pre&gt;&lt;code&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;cargo&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;add&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;tauri@2.0.0-alpha.4&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;cargo&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;add&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;tauri-build@2.0.0-alpha.2&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;--build&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;cargo&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;install&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;tauri-cli&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;--version&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;&quot;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;^2.0.0-alpha&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;&quot;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;--locked&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/figure&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;starlight-tabs-restore&gt;&lt;/starlight-tabs-restore&gt;&lt;/starlight-tabs&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Recreate the mobile projects to use the new features:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;figure&gt;&lt;figcaption&gt;&lt;span&gt;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/figcaption&gt;&lt;pre&gt;&lt;code&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;rm&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;-r&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;src-tauri/gen&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;tauri&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;android&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;init&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;tauri&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;ios&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;init&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/figure&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h2 id=&quot;http-client-breaking-change&quot;&gt;HTTP Client Breaking Change&lt;/h2&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The default HTTP client using &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;attohttpc&lt;/code&gt; has been removed due to issues with the development server proxy on Windows. All &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;reqwest-*&lt;/code&gt; feature flags have been removed because &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;reqwest&lt;/code&gt; is now the client we use.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;h2 id=&quot;native-mobile-functionality-for-tauri-plugins&quot;&gt;Native Mobile Functionality for Tauri Plugins&lt;/h2&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;A Tauri plugin now can access iOS via Swift and Android APIs via Kotlin or Java code, simplifying usage of platform interfaces such as camera or geolocation. To bootstrap the iOS and Android projects on an existing plugin, run &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri plugin ios add&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code dir=&quot;auto&quot;&gt;tauri plugin android add&lt;/code&gt;. New plugins automatically include all the configuration needed to write native mobile code.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Here’s an example of a plugin that takes a string value and resolves an object:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Android plugin:&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div&gt;&lt;figure&gt;&lt;figcaption&gt;&lt;span&gt;ExamplePlugin.kt&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/figcaption&gt;&lt;pre&gt;&lt;code&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;package&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; com.plugin.example&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;
&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;import&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; android.app.Activity&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;import&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; app.tauri.annotation.Command&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;import&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; app.tauri.annotation.TauriPlugin&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;import&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; app.tauri.plugin.JSObject&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;import&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; app.tauri.plugin.Plugin&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;import&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; app.tauri.plugin.Invoke&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;
&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;@TauriPlugin&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;class&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; ExamplePlugin(&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;private&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;val&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; activity: Activity): Plugin(activity) {&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;&lt;span&gt;    &lt;/span&gt;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;@Command&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;    &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;fun&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;ping&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;(invoke: Invoke) {&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;        &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;val&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;value&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;=&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; invoke.&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;getString&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;(&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;&quot;value&quot;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;) ?: &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;&quot;&quot;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;        &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;val&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; ret &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;=&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt; &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;JSObject&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span&gt;()&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;div&gt;&lt;span&gt;&lt;span&gt;
